verified for block 0 only, and the chain is not verified at all - the gate's inputs come from the oracle's seed, not
from block N-1's output.

BLOCK 1 FFN FIXTURE: THE GATE REFUSED IT, AND THE CAUSE IS ONE MEASUREMENT AWAY

    input/result are 8192/4096, expected 4096 each

The gate's size check rejected the fixture instead of gating on it, which is the correct behaviour and the reason the
check exists.  What is established: result.bin is 4096, and dense_forward asserts x.size == wg.shape[1] internally
without failing - so the x that was USED was 4096.  Yet input.bin on disk is 8192.  So the file and the value that
was passed to the oracle did not come from the same expression, which cannot be resolved by reasoning about the
script and needs one measurement: print x.size, the dump file's byte count, its header ne, and the destination
file's size, immediately before and after the write.

WHY THIS IS WORTH ONE LINE RATHER THAN A HYPOTHESIS.  Every fixture defect in this session has been a discrepancy
between what a file holds and what something else assumed it holds - a container header read as data, per-site
(view) slices taken for packed arrays, sizes derived instead of measured (five of them wrong by up to 4096x), and a
hand-rolled conversion that produced a fixture whose parts did not imply each other.  Writing the sizes down as the
file is created is the cheap habit that would have caught all of them at the point of creation, and it is the same
lesson as the aggregate check in split_raw_fixture.py, which DID catch five wrong sizes before a byte was written.
Make the FFN fixture builder do the same: assert the byte count it writes equals what it intended.

STATE: blocks 0, 1, 2 and 4 pass the KDA gate 12/12.  Block 0 passes the FFN gate (PASS at the fp32 floor, with the
clamp verified by a demonstrated failure) and the hc_post gate (2.551e-07).  The block-1 FFN fixture is blocked on
the size discrepancy above; nothing else about it is in question, and the weights for it dumped at the expected sizes
(34,603,008 / 34,603,008 / 41,287,680 bytes, matching block 0's exactly).

BLOCK 1's FFN MATCHES THE ORACLE AT THE fp32 FLOOR, AND THE CLAMP IS HONESTLY REPORTED AS UNTESTED

    input.bin 16384 bytes = 4096 float32     <- the fix for the float64 write, confirmed by size
    gate pre-activations: max 3.60009   above the 10.0 limit: 0 of 12288
    engine rms 0.086581   oracle rms 0.0865809
    worst absolute difference 1.281e-06   relative to oracle rms 1.480e-05   elements off: 0
    FFN GATE: INCONCLUSIVE

1.480e-05 of rms is the same figure block 0 shows and the same figure the fp32-vs-fp64 floor produces anywhere in
this port, with zero elements outside tolerance - so block 1's FFN arithmetic, its 12288 intermediate and its three
matrix layouts are correct on block 1's own weights.  The gate returns INCONCLUSIVE because the maximum gate
pre-activation is 3.60 against a limit of 10, so the clamp does nothing on this input; a PASS would have been a
claim about arithmetic the comparison never reached.  The scaled-input trick used for block 0 works here too if the
clamp needs exercising on this block.

THE DEFECT, RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS A RECURRING SHAPE: x was float64 and written with a bare tofile, so input.bin
held 8-byte elements - 32768 bytes, which the gate read as 8192 float32s - while result.bin was written with an
explicit dtype and was correct.  The gate refused on the size instead of gating on a mismatched input, and the cause
was the TYPE, not the arithmetic.  This is the fourth instance in this session of a writer and a reader disagreeing
about format: a dump container header read as data, per-site (view) slices taken for packed arrays, five derived
array sizes wrong by up to 4096x, and now a float64 file read as float32.  The habit that catches all four is to
print the byte count actually written at creation time and assert it against the intent.

FFN GATE STATUS: block 0 PASS at the fp32 floor with the clamp verified by a demonstrated failure (clamp disabled
costs 15000x over 4092 elements); block 1 matches at the same floor with the clamp untested on a real activation.

########  THE CHAIN IS GLUE, NOT MATH - AND THAT IS THE ACTIONABLE FINDING  ########

src/core/glm_layer.cpp and its header contain EXACTLY ONE stage function:

    glm_stage_hc_norm    hc_pre + the host RMS norm, i.e. stage 1

So the engine cannot execute a whole block yet, and the chain - block N's output feeding block N+1's input - is not
one function away.  But every piece the assembly needs already exists and has been independently verified:

    hc_pre          verified against the oracle to 7 significant digits (block 0)
    the RMS norm    5.4e-10
    kda_forward     PASS on blocks 0, 1, 2 and 4, 12/12 stages each, plus T=5 on block 0
    hc_post         2.551e-07, the tightest agreement in the port
    expert_ffn      block 0 PASS at the fp32 floor with the clamp verified by a demonstrated failure;
                    block 1 matches at the same floor
    the binding     16/16 KDA weights bound to oracle values, plus every FFN tensor now readable as floats

So the remaining work on block 0's chain is ASSEMBLY: calling the pieces in the right order with the right residual
routing, and no new arithmetic.  That is a very different proposition from "the chain is unverified", and it is the
single most useful thing to know before starting it.

THE ORDER, from the reference and confirmed by the dump's own per-block stage names: each block has TWO
hyper-connection sites, which is why the dump carries both hc_attn_pre-N and hc_ffn_pre-N for every N -

    x -> hc_pre (attn site)  -> attn_norm -> attention (KDA, or MLA at block 3)
      -> hc_post (attn site) -> hc_pre (ffn site) -> ffn_norm -> FFN (dense below block 3, MoE at and above)
      -> hc_post (ffn site)  -> the block's output, which is the next block's input

The two hc sites are why hc_attn_fn/base/scale and hc_ffn_fn/base/scale both exist per block, and why the dump has
hc_ffn_pre-N alongside hc_attn_pre-N.  The gate fixtures built so far exercise the ATTN site only, which is worth
remembering: hc_post, for instance, was verified with the attn site's mix.

NEXT: implement the block assembly in glm_layer.cpp against that order, then verify it stage by stage against the
dump's per-block activations - which exist for all 45 blocks and were read successfully for block 1 (attn_norm-1,
ne=[4096,5,1,1]), so the chain can be gated one stage at a time rather than only end to end.

########  THE DUMP CARRIES EVERY STAGE OF THE CHAIN, INCLUDING THE BLOCK OUTPUT  ########

Distinct per-block families in glm5-ref-dump5, block 0:

    l_out          the BLOCK OUTPUT                    attn_output    the attention's output
    hc_attn_pre    the attn site's hc_pre              hc_attn_post   the attn site's hc_post
    hc_ffn_pre     the ffn site's hc_pre               ffn_out        the FFN's output
    attn_norm      the attention's RMS norm            ffn_norm       the FFN's RMS norm
    kda_gate, kda_beta                                 hc_attn_base/scale, hc_ffn_base/scale

So the reference data covers the ENTIRE block sequence, not just isolated stages:

    hc_attn_pre -> attn_norm -> [attention] -> attn_output -> hc_attn_post
      -> hc_ffn_pre -> ffn_norm -> ffn_out -> l_out

Two consequences that together make the chain tractable:

  1. IT CAN BE GATED STAGE BY STAGE.  Every step has expected values, so a partially assembled block can be checked
     at each point rather than only when finished - which matters because a chain failure is otherwise hard to
     localise, and this port has just spent hours learning what that costs.

  2. THE CHAIN'S INPUTS EXIST PER BLOCK.  l_out-N is block N's output and therefore block N+1's input, so the real
     hidden state is available for every block without running anything.  A gate can feed block N+1 its true input
     rather than the oracle's seed, which is what separates "the kernel handles these weights" from "the block
     computes the right answer from the real state" - the distinction this file has been careful to draw.

Note also attn_output-0 has (cont) and (cont) (reshaped) variants, i.e. the token-major layout with continuations,
the same shape convention as attn_norm-N.  Read the header, as always: hc_attn_pre-1 measured ne=[4096,5,1,1].

WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE ASSEMBLY WORK: implement the order above in glm_layer.cpp, then check each stage against the
matching dump family, then check l_out against the next block's real input by feeding it back in.  No new arithmetic
and no new reference data are needed - both halves already exist.

########  CHAIN ASSEMBLY BEGUN: glm_stage_kda, THE SECOND OF THE BLOCK'S STAGES  ########

src/core/glm_layer.cpp now holds two stages:

    glm_stage_hc_norm   hc_pre + the site's RMS norm        (stage 1, previously verified)
    glm_stage_kda       the attention call                 (NEW: binds the KDA weight views and calls kda_forward)

glm_stage_kda contains no arithmetic by design: for a KDA block, stage 1's output is what kda_forward consumes
directly, so the function is the wiring that lets a chain call the verified kernel with a block's own bound weights.
Its contract notes that `state` (the recurrent S matrix, nh*hd*hd floats) must be zeroed for a fresh sequence -
every gate fixture so far is T=1 or a fresh T=5, so nothing carries state across calls yet, and a real sequential
decode is a different test.

THREE FILTERED VIEWS MISLED ME IN A ROW, and the fix was the same each time - a check that cannot be satisfied by an
absence:

  * the first build printed no error lines, which I read as success.  It had built a target that does not contain
    glm_layer.cpp at all; the tell was "ninja: no work to do" after a touch, which I passed over;
  * the second build printed "collect2: error: ld returned 1 exit status" while my grep, searching for "error:",
    hid the undefined-reference line that said exactly what was wrong;
  * only nm -C ... | grep -c glm_stage_kda settled it: 0 symbols in strata_core (the file is not in that target),
    then 2 in glm5_bind_check once src/kernels/glm_kda.cpp was added to it.

The lesson generalises: when the question is "did this compile", a symbol count answers it and an absence of error
text does not.

A USEFUL SIDE EFFECT: the linker's undefined-reference message printed the full demangled signature of kda_forward,
which independently confirms the new stage calls it with the right seven arguments, right types, right namespace.

STATE: the block has 2 of its 8 stages wired (hc_norm, kda).  Next in the order: hc_post (attn site), then the ffn
site's hc_pre + norm, expert_ffn, and the final hc_post.  Every one of those already exists as verified code.

CHAIN ASSEMBLY: 3 STAGES WIRED, AND ONLY ONE NEW ONE REMAINS

    glm_stage_hc_norm   stage 1 (previously verified)     glm_stage_hc_post   a verified kernel call
    glm_stage_kda       the attention call                glm_stage_ffn       the FFN call (added this round)

The count matters more than the individual additions: the FFN site needs hc_pre + its norm, which IS glm_stage_hc_norm,
and then hc_post, which IS glm_stage_hc_post.  So the block's eight-step sequence needs only four stage functions,
two of which are new.  Everything is a call into an already-verified kernel; no arithmetic has been added anywhere.

DATA FLOW, which alternates shape and is the easiest thing to get wrong silently because both are float*:
    x                  [HC][n_embd]   the block's input streams
    hc_pre  -> layer_in [n_embd]      one vector per token
    norm    -> xn       [n_embd]
    attention -> attn_output [n_embd]
    hc_post ->         [HC][n_embd]   HC rows again, because the next site's hc_pre consumes exactly that
    ... repeated for the FFN site ...
    l_out              [HC][n_embd]   the block's output, and therefore the next block's input

VERIFICATION DISCIPLINE, now standard: a symbol count after every addition, because this session has three times
read an absence of error output as success - a target that did not contain the file, a grep that hid the error it was
searching for, and a truncated buffer.  nm -C ... | grep -c answers the question; nothing else does.

########  ALL FOUR STAGE FUNCTIONS EXIST - THE REMAINING WORK IS THE BLOCK LOOP  ########

    [4/4] Linking CXX executable glm5_bind_check
    chain stage symbols in the binary: 4

glm_stage_hc_norm, glm_stage_kda, glm_stage_hc_post, glm_stage_ffn - four functions, eight steps, and no arithmetic
added anywhere.  The block's sequence and the buffers it needs, which is the specification the loop must follow:

    in:   x            [HC][n_embd]  (HC=4 streams; a new sequence starts from the embedding, so this is [4][n_embd])
    1  glm_stage_hc_norm(x, hc_attn_fn, hc_attn_base, hc_attn_scale, attn_norm_w, layer_in, &mix_attn)
         -> layer_in [n_embd]  and mix_attn (which hc_post needs)
    2  attn_norm: the same function already applied norm_w, so layer_in here IS xn  -> [n_embd]
    3  glm_stage_kda(xn, kda_weights, kda_geometry, tokens, attn_output, state)      -> [n_embd]
    4  glm_stage_hc_post(attn_output, x, mix_attn, n_embd, mid)                     -> [HC][n_embd]
    5  glm_stage_hc_norm(mid, hc_ffn_fn, hc_ffn_base, hc_ffn_scale, ffn_norm_w, ffn_in, &mix_ffn)
         -> ffn_in [n_embd] and mix_ffn
    6  glm_stage_ffn(ffn_in, ffn_gate, ffn_up, ffn_down, geom, ffn_out, clamp_shexp) -> [n_embd]
    7  glm_stage_hc_post(ffn_out, mid, mix_ffn, n_embd, l_out)                      -> [HC][n_embd] = the block output

Note step 2: glm_stage_hc_norm applies the site's norm internally, so there is no separate norm stage - what the
signature calls `layer_in` is already normed by the time it returns.  That is worth checking against the dump, since
the dump has BOTH hc_attn_pre-N and attn_norm-N as separate quantities; if the function's output matches attn_norm-N
then the naming is just historical, and if it matches hc_attn_pre-N then a norm step is missing from the loop.

VERIFICATION PLAN, now that the dump provides every stage: run the loop for block 0 and compare each intermediate
against its dump family - hc_attn_pre-N, attn_norm-N, attn_output-N, hc_attn_post-N, hc_ffn_pre-N, ffn_norm-N,
ffn_out-N, l_out-N.  Then feed l_out-0 back in as block 1's input and check the chain, which is the distinction this
file has been careful to draw all along: kernel-handles-weights versus block-computes-the-right-answer-from-the-real
state.

WHY THIS IS A GOOD PLACE TO STOP: the four stage functions are additive and independently verified in the binary, and
they change no existing behaviour.  The loop is the first piece of the chain with real logic - the routing in steps 4
and 7, and the shape alternation - so it deserves a fresh context rather than the tail of a long one.  Nothing is
half-written: every function present compiles, links, and is a call into a kernel with its own gate.

THE OPEN QUESTION IS CLOSED: glm_stage_hc_norm RETURNS THE NORMED VALUE

    if (norm_w) {
        double ss = 0; for (i) ss += layer_in[i]^2;
        const float rms = 1/sqrt(ss/n_embd + eps);
        for (i) layer_in[i] = layer_in[i] * rms * norm_w[i];       // IN PLACE
    }

So the same function yields either of the dump's two quantities depending on the argument:
    norm_w = the site's norm weight   -> layer_in becomes attn_norm-N    (normed)
    norm_w = nullptr                  -> layer_in remains hc_attn_pre-N  (pre-norm)
which is how the driver printed both, and it means the seven-call loop spec needs NO separate norm step.  The
ambiguity that worried me was in the naming, not in the arithmetic - and it was worth resolving before building on
it, because a missing or duplicated norm is exactly the kind of "wrong against wrong" that this port has already been
bitten by once.

That closes the last unknown in the block loop.  The spec stands as recorded: seven calls, four stage functions,
with the norm folded into the first of each site pair.

NEXT AND ONLY NEXT: write the loop.  It is fully specified - the call sequence, the buffer shapes and the residual
routing are all recorded above - and the dump provides an expected value for every intermediate, so it can be gated
stage by stage rather than only end to end.  What it needs beyond the loop itself is a driver that runs it for block 0
and prints each intermediate's statistics beside its dump family, in the same shape kda_gate and ffn_gate already
use; then feed l_out-0 in as block 1's input and check the chain.

THE LOOP'S REMAINING QUESTION IS AN INTERFACE, NOT AN ALGORITHM

The seven calls and their buffers are fully specified.  What is undecided is how the loop RECEIVES the block's
weights: it needs roughly fifteen pointers (hc_attn_fn/base/scale, attn_norm, the sixteen KDA weights, hc_ffn_*,
ffn_norm, and the FFN's three matrices), and the choice determines the code that follows rather than being a detail
inside it.  Two candidates, with what each costs:

  (a) a plain struct of pointers, filled by the caller.  Explicit, no lookup, trivially testable with the fixture
      data the gates already load, and it keeps glm_layer.cpp free of any dependency on the binding.  Cost: a
      fifteen-field struct that the caller must fill correctly, so a driver has to be written alongside it.

  (b) take the bound block (GlmBoundBlock from glm_bind.hpp) and look tensors up by name.  Less plumbing for a
      driver, because the driver already binds a block - but the names are BARE ("attn_q.weight"), which has bitten
      this port once already, and it couples the chain to the binding layer, so the loop could no longer be tested
      against fixture files without a bound artifact.

(a) is the better fit for how everything here has been verified: every gate in this port loads plain arrays from
files, so a struct-of-pointers loop can be driven by exactly those arrays.  (b) would make the chain testable only
end-to-end through the loader, which is the arrangement that has cost this port the most time.

RECOMMENDATION: (a), with the struct defined in glm_layer.hpp next to the stage declarations, and a driver that fills
it from the fixture files already on disk - the same arrays kda_gate and ffn_gate load - so the loop's first run is
gated against the dump stage by stage rather than only as a whole.

WHY THIS IS THE STOPPING POINT AND NOT ANOTHER ADDITIVE EDIT: every step that went well this session was additive and
build-verified - a new stage function, a new tool, a new fixture.  This is different: it is a decision that shapes the
next several edits, and choosing it in the tail of a long context is how this session's earlier rewrites happened.
The stage functions are all committed, linked and counted; nothing is half-written.

########  THE BLOCK LOOP EXISTS: glm_block_forward  ########

Chain symbols in glm5_bind_check after adding it: 6, where four stages plus the loop should be 5.  Named rather than
waved through - a count that disagrees with the prediction is information, not noise, and the two extra entries are
worth knowing about before anything is built on top of them.  (See the line below for what they were.)

WHAT WAS ADDED, and it is the whole block:

    glm_block_forward(x, tokens, GlmBlockWeights, hc_rms_eps, l_out, state, stream, err)

    hc_pre -> attn_norm -> attention -> hc_post -> hc_pre -> ffn_norm -> FFN -> hc_post

    x      [HC][n_embd]  in        l_out  [HC][n_embd]  out = the next block's input
    per token: the attention and FFN outputs are [n_embd], everything between the sites is [HC][n_embd]

THE INTERFACE CHOICE, made and recorded: GlmBlockWeights is a struct of plain pointers rather than the binding layer's
structures, so the loop can be driven by the same fixture arrays every gate in this port already loads - and
glm_layer.cpp stays independent of the loader.  The struct carries the FFN geometry (ff = 12288 dense vs 2048 routed)
and the clamp limit, since both differ by block kind and neither is derivable from the weights.

KNOWN LIMITATION, stated in the code: the loop runs the attention per token with tokens=1.  That is correct here
because the KDA recurrence is sequential anyway, so batching the matmuls would not help - but it does mean the block
is not a batched implementation, and the state is threaded through one call per token rather than per sequence.

NOT YET DONE, and it is the next thing: nothing has RUN the loop.  The dump provides an expected value for every
intermediate (hc_attn_pre, attn_norm, attn_output, hc_attn_post, hc_ffn_pre, ffn_norm, ffn_out, l_out), so the first
run should be a driver that fills GlmBlockWeights from those fixture files, runs block 0, and prints each
intermediate beside its dump family - in the shape kda_gate and ffn_gate already use.  Then feed l_out-0 back in as
block 1's input, which is the check this whole session has been building toward.

THE SIXTH SYMBOL, EXPLAINED: a compiler-generated cold clone, not a fifth stage

    T glm_stage_ffn / glm_stage_kda / glm_stage_hc_norm / glm_stage_hc_post / glm_block_forward
    t glm_block_forward [clone .cold]

Five real functions plus one out-of-line cold path the compiler emitted for the loop's error branches.  So the
expected count was right and the naming was the thing to do - a count that disagrees with a prediction is a question,
and the answer here is benign, but it was worth the one command rather than an assumption either way.

BONUS: the demangled names independently confirm every interface written this session - glm_stage_kda takes
(KdaWeights const&, KdaGeometry const&, int, float*, float*, string&), glm_stage_ffn takes
(MoeGeometry const&, float*, float, string&), and glm_block_forward takes (float const*, int, GlmBlockWeights const&,
float, float*, float*, void*, string&).  The linker is a check on what was written, not on what was intended.

STATE, and it is the first time this can be said: the chain exists as code.  hc_pre -> attn_norm -> attention ->
hc_post -> hc_pre -> ffn_norm -> FFN -> hc_post, with the residual routing, as one function.  Nothing has run it yet -
its four constituent kernels each have passing gates, but the assembly has no test - and the dump supplies an expected
value for every intermediate, so the first run is a driver that fills GlmBlockWeights from fixture files and prints
each stage beside its dump family.

########  THE BLOCK FIXTURE IS COMPLETE - EVERY INPUT THE LOOP NEEDS EXISTS ON DISK  ########

    w_hc_attn_fn      393,216 floats   [-0.37885, 0.39847]     w_hc_ffn_fn      393,216   [-0.36141, 0.40429]
    w_hc_attn_base         24 floats   [-8, 2.6923]            w_hc_ffn_base         24   [-8.2931, 0.13325]
    w_hc_attn_scale         3 floats   [0.044621, 0.088875]    w_hc_ffn_scale         3   [0.056572, 0.11865]
    w_attn_norm         4,096 floats   [0.070312, 0.15723]     w_ffn_norm         4,096   [0.028198, 0.15723]
    x.bin               4 streams x 4096, from hc_init         = 16,384 floats

with the 16 KDA weights in glm5-kda-l0-t1/ and the FFN's three raw block sets in glm5-ffn-l0/, and every expected
stage readable from glm5-ref-dump5/.  So nothing is missing for a block-0 run except the driver itself.

A FREE CONSISTENCY CHECK IN THE SIZES: hc_*_fn is 393,216 = 4096 x 96, and 96 = 4 streams x 24 = 4 pre + 4 post + 16
comb - exactly the HcMix{pre[4], post[4], comb[4][4]} the engine's header declares.  The site's mix is a 96-wide
projection, and the artifact's own tensor size says so independently of the code.

THE SHAPE ASSERTION EARned ITS KEEP IMMEDIATELY, ON A SCRIPT WRITTEN MINUTES EARLIER.  The first attempt read x from
hc_attn_pre-0.bin, whose header is ne=[4096, 5, 1, 1].  I read ne[1] as a stream count and got 5; the assertion
refused.  In fact ne[1] is the TOKEN count there and the whole tensor is one stream over five tokens - the normed
layer input, not the block's stream input.  The correct source is hc_init, ne=[4096, 4, 5, 1]: four streams, five
tokens.  Both are float*, both are plausible sizes, and nothing downstream would have noticed - the block loop would
simply have produced a wrong answer whose blame would have fallen on kernel code that is already verified.

That is now the sixth time in this port that a writer and a reader have had to agree about a format and only an
assertion could tell them apart: a dump container header read as data, per-site (view) slices taken for packed
arrays, five derived array sizes wrong by up to 4096x, a float64 file read as float32, and now a single-stream array
taken for the stream set.  The rule that catches all six: assert the shape you expect rather than accepting the one
you are given.

NEXT AND LAST: the driver.  It fills GlmBlockWeights from glm5-block-l0 (the eight weights), the KDA fixture (the
sixteen), and the FFN fixture (the three raw block sets, through the two proven dequantizers), reads x.bin, runs
glm_block_forward for one token, and prints each stage beside its dump family - hc_attn_pre, attn_norm, attn_output,
hc_attn_post, hc_ffn_pre, ffn_norm, ffn_out, l_out.  Nothing has run the loop yet.

########  block_gate: 29 OF 29 INPUTS SHAPE-VERIFIED - AND THE TOOL'S FIRST RUN CAUGHT 10 WRONG ASSUMPTIONS  ########

    block_gate: 29 of 29 inputs loaded and shape-checked  -> fixture is loadable and shape-consistent
    NOTE: this version loads only.  The run and the per-stage comparison against the dump come next -
          the loop has not been executed yet, and loading being right does not make it right.

The tool is load-and-check only, deliberately: nothing computes on a fixture until the fixture is proven
shape-consistent.  That decision paid for itself on the very first run, which reported 10 of 29 inputs BAD and
refused to proceed.  Every one was a size I had INFERRED from the geometry rather than measured:

    w_wq        134,217,728 B  vs my 268,435,456   I assumed 2x; it is ne x d_inner = 4096 x 8192
    w_ssm_a             256 B  vs my 32,768        it is per-head (nh=64), not d_inner
    w_ssm_f_a     2,097,152 B  vs my 32,768        d_inner x 64
    w_ssm_f_b     4,194,304 B  vs my 32,768        d_inner x 128
    w_ssm_beta    1,048,576 B  vs my 32,768        d_inner x 32
    MISSING w_ssm_dt / w_ssm_norm / w_input_norm   the fixture names them dt_bias and o_norm, and has no
                                                   separate input_norm because attn_norm serves that role

Errors of 2x to 128x.  Any of them would have produced a plausible wrong answer whose blame would have fallen on
kernel code that already has passing gates - the most expensive kind of false lead this port has produced.  The
sizes now come from listing the fixture and dividing by four, because the oracle's writer is the authority on what
it emits, not my reading of the geometry.

FOUR INSTRUMENTS, AND WHAT EACH CAUGHT:

  * the load-and-check STRUCTURE caught the 10 wrong sizes, by refusing to run rather than proceeding;
  * the compiler caught six guessed identifiers at once (nh, hd, d_inner() - not n_heads, head_dim, MoE_dense);
  * the patch tool fixed the table in ONE call after four rounds of python string surgery - all four of which
    aborted, not on the edit, but on my own over-strict asserts: a bare "n_heads" inside a printf LABEL, then a
    bare "ssm_dt" inside a COMMENT I had just written, then a count of `{"attn_norm"` that is legitimately 2
    because attn_norm appears in two tables.  The rule: assert on the string you are CHANGING, never on the bare
    word, and prefer the patch tool over string surgery for files with repeated identifiers;
  * the LSP diagnostics were right twice, after I had spent the session treating them as noise - a placeholder I
    had left in (ne_hack) and a declaration the patch duplicated.  Reading them costs nothing.

STATE: the fixture is proven, the loop is written and links, and NOTHING HAS RUN IT.  The remaining work is the
run plus the per-stage comparison - the driver half of this tool, comparing hc_attn_pre, attn_norm, attn_output,
hc_attn_post, hc_ffn_pre, ffn_norm, ffn_out and l_out against their dump families, then feeding l_out-0 back in as
block 1's input.

########  THE REMAINING WORK, SPECIFIED SO THE NEXT STEP IS MECHANICAL  ########

The goal is NOT complete: nothing has run glm_block_forward, and loading being right does not make the loop right.
block_gate's first half is done and proven (29/29 shape-checked).  What remains is the run and the comparison, and
it is the only thing between this port and a verified chain.

IN block_gate.cpp, after the loading section passes its checks, build the weights and run:

  (1) GlmBlockWeights w;  fill from the arrays already loaded in the three sections above -
        from the block dir:  hc_attn_fn, hc_attn_base, hc_attn_scale, attn_norm,
                             hc_ffn_fn, hc_ffn_base, hc_ffn_scale, ffn_norm
        from the KDA dir:    the sixteen arrays into a KdaWeights (that struct's fields are declared at
                             include/strata/kernels/glm_kda.hpp:40 - READ THEM, do not infer the names: this session
                             lost four rounds to guessed identifiers);
        from the FFN dir:    three matrix buffers, filled by dequantizing raw_gate/raw_up with dequant_q5_K and
                             raw_down with dequant_q6_K - exactly what tools/ffn_gate.cpp already does, so copy that
                             call pattern rather than inventing one;
        geometry:            KdaGeometry g (defaults are correct for this model); MoeGeometry fg with ff = 12288 for
                             the leading dense block, w_scale/norm_w untouched, and clamp_limit = 10.0 (shexp);
        plus:                kda = &kw, kda_geom = &g, ffn_geom = &fg.
  (2) buffers: x from the loaded x.bin (HC x n_embd = 16,384 floats), l_out HC x n_embd, and a state of
      nh*hd*hd = 64*128*128 = 1,048,576 floats ZEROED - the recurrence is sequential, so it must start at zero.
  (3) call glm_block_forward(x, 1, w, 1e-5f, l_out, state, nullptr, err) and report err if it returns false.
  (4) compare: the loop's intermediates are internal, so for a first run compare what it returns - l_out - against
      the dump's l_out-0, and ALSO check the shape: l_out should be ne=4 streams x 4096.  Then, to localise any
      disagreement rather than only detecting it, drive the four stages directly in the same order and compare each
      against its dump family: hc_attn_pre-N (from hc_norm with norm_w=nullptr), attn_norm-N (with norm_w), then
      attn_output-N after the KDA call, then hc_attn_post-N, then hc_ffn_pre-N, ffn_norm-N, ffn_out-N, l_out-N.
      Every one of those has an expected value on disk.
  (5) after block 0 passes, feed l_out-0 back as block 1's input and repeat with block 1's weights - the check this
      whole session has been building toward, because it tests the block against the REAL hidden state rather than
      against a seed the loop was given.

READ THE STRUCT FIELDS BEFORE WRITING THEM.  Every identifier in this port has a declared name that is not always
the intuitive one: kda uses nh / hd / d_inner() (not n_heads / head_dim / d_inner), the fixture names two arrays
dt_bias and o_norm, and hc_*_fn is 4096 x 96 rather than 4096 x 24.  Guessing cost six compiler errors and four
failed edits this session; grep -A14 'struct <Name>' costs one command.

EXPECT THE FIRST RUN TO DISAGREE SOMEWHERE.  The loop has never executed, and the likely places are the residual
routing at the two hc_post stages and the shape alternation between [n_embd] and [HC][n_embd] - both are float*, so a
mistake there compiles and runs and produces wrong numbers rather than an error.  That is exactly why the comparison
is per stage as well as end to end.

KdaWeights AS DECLARED, AND WHY NO MAPPING TABLE IS NEEDED

    include/strata/kernels/glm_kda.hpp:40

    attn_norm  [n_embd]          wq/wk/wv  [d_inner][n_embd]     conv_q/k/v  [d_conv][d_inner]
    ssm_a      [nh]  holds -exp(A_log)                          dt_bias     [d_inner]
    ssm_f_a    [hd][n_embd]      ssm_f_b   [d_inner][hd]
    ssm_beta   [nh][n_embd]      ssm_g_a   [hd][n_embd]          ssm_g_b     [d_inner][hd]
    o_norm     [hd], the gain of ssm_norm                       wo          [n_embd][d_inner]

Every size measured off the fixture matches one of those declared shapes exactly:

    524,288 = hd x n_embd = 128 x 4096      (f_a, g_a)
  1,048,576 = d_inner x hd = 8192 x 128     (f_b, g_b)
    262,144 = nh x n_embd = 64 x 4096       (beta)
         64 = nh                            (ssm_a)
      8,192 = d_inner                         (dt_bias)
        128 = hd                              (o_norm)

So the "x64 / x128 / x32" sizes I first wrote were hd and n_embd in disguise, and the fixture's FILE names are the
struct's FIELD names - dt_bias, o_norm, ssm_a, attn_norm and the rest.  The fill is therefore a mechanical loop over
the sixteen, with no mapping table and no renaming: load w_<field>.bin into <field>.  That is worth having checked
rather than assumed, because the two names that differ in the ARTIFACT (ssm_bias for dt_bias, o_norm for ssm_norm) are
a different layer from the fixture, and this port has already been bitten once by assuming a name carried across.

Also declared and useful: KDA_RMS_EPS = 1e-5f (attention.layer_norm_rms_epsilon), KDA_L2_EPS = 1e-6f, and
KDA_GATE_LOWER = -5.0f, each named after the metadata key it comes from so the two eps cannot be swapped.

The remaining work is unchanged and still unstarted: fill GlmBlockWeights, allocate x/l_out and a zeroed
nh*hd*hd = 1,048,576-float state, call glm_block_forward, compare l_out and then each stage against the dump, then
feed l_out-0 back in as block 1's input.  With the field names now known to be identical to the fixture's, step 1 is
a loop rather than a table, which is the last thing that could have been got wrong in the loading.

########  THE ATTENTION SITE EXECUTES - AND THE COMPARISON IS BLOCKED BY THE DUMP, NOT BY A BUG  ########

block_gate now runs the attention site end to end and compares four stages.  All four stages EXECUTE (every call
returns true); the comparison reports four FAILs, and the numbers say why.

    hc_attn_pre   worst 1.760e-05   of dump rms 4.125e-03   -> 4.266e-03
    attn_norm     worst 1.121e-04   of dump rms 8.094e-02   -> 1.385e-03
    attn_output   worst 2.586e-02   of dump rms 2.233e-04   -> 1.158e+02
    hc_attn_post  worst 1.364e-02   of dump rms 5.660e-03   -> 2.409e+00

FIRST, THE SLICING IS RIGHT.  The dump's hc_attn_pre-0 slice 0 measures min -0.0154 / max 0.0215 / rms 0.004125, and
the contract's own record of that dump node is min -0.0154028935 max 0.0215204563 - identical.  So token 0 is the
leading slice of each family, as the container layout says, and no alignment error remains in the reading.

SECOND, AND THIS IS THE BLOCKER: the dump and the ORACLE disagree at attn_norm.  The engine's stage-1 output is
min -0.359747499 / max 0.424176991, which was verified against the phase-6 oracle to seven significant digits.  The
dump's attn_norm-0 token 0 is min -0.2749 / max 0.37, and NO slice of that file matches the oracle - the five slices
run -0.2749/0.37, -0.3766/0.3435, -0.4941/0.4567, -0.3209/0.298, -0.2641/0.2757.  So the engine agrees with the oracle
and disagrees with the dump, at a stage whose arithmetic has been independently verified.  This is the item ANOTHER
part of this contract already flagged as unresolved: what hc_attn_pre-N actually holds, where the discrepancy is
between the dump node and the oracle rather than in the engine.  The per-stage comparison has now made it precise.

THIRD, EVIDENCE THE DUMP'S attn_output IS A DIFFERENT QUANTITY.  Its token-0 slice has rms 2.23e-04 with min -0.0186
and max +0.00157 - an ASYMMETRY of 12x between the extremes, where a projected attention output should be roughly
symmetric.  And its rms is 370x smaller than its own input's 0.081, which a projection through wo does not do.  So
this family is not the same quantity the stage produces, and comparing against it tests the dump's semantics rather
than the engine's arithmetic.

WHAT THIS MEANS: the attention site RUNS, and produces every stage.  Its magnitudes are right (hc_attn_pre 4e-3,
attn_norm 0.08 - the norm divides by an rms of ~0.004 and multiplies by weights of ~0.1, so 0.08 follows).  What it
cannot yet do is CHECK itself against these dump families, because at least two of them hold something else.

THE DECISIVE NEXT TEST: compare against the ORACLE rather than the dump.  kda_gate already verified block 0's KDA
12/12, but it fed the KERNEL the oracle's own seed; this run feeds it hc_init's real token.  So the next step is to
run the Python oracle on the same real input - hc_init's token 0 through hc_pre and the norm, then kda_block - and
compare the engine's four stages against the oracle's, which removes the dump's semantics from the question entirely.
That is one script using code that already exists, and it is the only way to separate "the engine is wrong" from
"the dump is a different quantity" at these stages.

########  A REAL FINDING: THE KDA KERNEL DISAGREES WITH THE ORACLE ON A REAL INPUT, 1.391x  ########

block_gate feeds the KDA the ORACLE's own attn_norm - byte-identical input, same weights, same eps - and the output
is still 39% larger than the oracle's:

    run: the KDA is fed the ORACLE's attn_norm, so any remaining difference is the kernel
    FAIL  attn_output  worst 7.383e-03  ref rms 0.00450658  engine rms 0.00626758  ratio 1.391

with the worst absolute difference UNCHANGED to four significant digits from the run where the engine used its own
norm (7.383e-03 both times).  That is itself evidence: replacing the input with the oracle's changed nothing, because
the engine's stage-1 norm and the oracle's agree to 1.4e-03 of rms - so the 0.14% input difference was never capable
of producing the 39% output difference, and the disagreement is NOT inherited from upstream.

THE BISECTION, which is what made this unambiguous:

    hc_attn_pre   worst 1.760e-05   ref rms 4.125e-03   engine 4.122e-03   ratio 0.999   -> 4.266e-03
    attn_norm     worst 1.121e-04   ref rms 8.094e-02   engine 8.092e-02   ratio 1.000   -> 1.384e-03
    attn_output   worst 7.383e-03   ref rms 4.507e-03   engine 6.268e-03   ratio 1.391   -> 1.638e+00

Stage 1 is sound: both quantities match the oracle's to 0.1% in magnitude.  The KDA is where it breaks, and it breaks
by a FACTOR rather than by noise - 1.391 is a scale relationship, not a rounding error, and a factor is what a missing
or duplicated operation produces.

WHY THIS IS A REAL FINDING AND NOT A REGRESSION: kda_gate passes block 0 twelve of twelve at T=1 and T=5.  But it
feeds the KERNEL the oracle's own fixture seed.  Here the kernel gets a real activation, and it disagrees.  So the
existing gate does not cover this case - which is exactly the distinction this whole session has been drawing, now
demonstrated on the engine's own code rather than on a fixture.

THE NEXT STEP IS THE SAME TECHNIQUE ONE LEVEL DOWN: kda_gate already compares TWELVE intermediates (xn, qc, kc, vc, g,
beta, q, k, v, attn, o, result) against the oracle.  Run that comparison on THIS input - the oracle's attn_norm for
token 0 of block 0 - and the first deviating intermediate names the operation at fault.  Candidates worth having in
mind, in the order the operations run and all of which produce a FACTOR rather than a shift if wrong: the L2
normalisation of q and k (L2_EPS 1e-6, hard-coded in the reference), the gate's exp and the log-decay from ssm_a
(which holds -exp(A_log)), the beta sigmoid, the per-head ssm_norm gain o_norm, and the final wo projection.  A 39%
overshoot with the correct sign structure and the right order of magnitude points at a scale in one of those, not at a
layout error - a layout error would typically decorrelate the output rather than scale it.

ANALYSIS CAUGHT ONE THING WORTH RECORDING: the first version of this patch assigned the oracle's attn_norm INTO the
buffer that the attn_norm comparison reads, which would have made stage 1 compare the oracle against itself - a gate
that cannot fail, and precisely the wrong-against-wrong defect this port spent a session on.  It was changed to a
separate kda_in buffer.  The check that caught it was following the data one step further than the edit.

HOW TO GET THE TWELVE-INTERMEDIATE COMPARISON ON A REAL INPUT - the exact recipe, so the next step is mechanical

The oracle's own raw-fixture writer (glm5_kda_reference.py, in main() at the --raw-fixture branch) emits, verbatim:

    fh.write(struct.pack("<6i", N_EMBD, NH, HD, D_CONV, tokens, layer))
    for arr in (w["attn_norm"], w["wq"], w["wk"], w["wv"], w["conv_q"], w["conv_k"], w["conv_v"],
                w["ssm_a"], w["dt_bias"], w["ssm_f_a"], w["ssm_f_b"], w["ssm_beta"],
                w["ssm_g_a"], w["ssm_g_b"], w["o_norm"], w["wo"], x):
        fh.write(np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.float32).tobytes())
    for arr in (mid["qc"], mid["kc"], mid["vc"], mid["g"], mid["beta"], mid["q"], mid["k"],
                mid["v"], mid["attn"], mid["o"], result):
        fh.write(np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.float32).tobytes())

with `result, mid = kda_block(w, x, tokens, want_state=True)`, and x shaped (T, D) - token major, because
conv1d_causal unpacks `T, ch = x.shape` and rejects a flat vector.

So the missing piece is a script that takes the oracle's attn_norm-0.bin token 0 as `x`, calls kda_block on it, and
writes that raw file; then existing, already-proven tools do the rest unchanged:

    1  oracle stages on this input  ->  raw fixture (the layout above)
    2  tools/split_raw_fixture.py RAW OUTDIR      (validates that the arrays consume the file exactly)
    3  ./build-glm5/kda_gate OUTDIR 1             (compares TWELVE intermediates against the oracle's)

and the FIRST intermediate that deviates names the operation at fault: xn, qc, kc, vc, g, beta, q, k, v, attn, o,
result - where xn is what split_raw_fixture derives as the normed input.

Reuse the splitter rather than reproducing the layout anywhere: it already asserts the byte totals, and this port has
lost more time to a hand-rolled fixture that did not imply its own outputs than to any other single cause.  The writer
above is copied verbatim so that the new script differs from the oracle's own writer only in where `x` comes from -
which is the whole point of the exercise, and the one difference that has to be right.

WHY THIS IS WORTH DOING NEXT AND IS THE LAST THING NEEDED: the kernel now has a demonstrated 1.391x disagreement with
the oracle on a real activation, while passing twelve of twelve on the oracle's seed.  The twelve-intermediate gate is
already built, already proven, and already has its failure mode demonstrated; pointing it at this input converts "the
kernel is wrong by a factor somewhere" into "this operation is wrong", in one run.

########  RETRACTION: THE 1.391 WAS NOT A KERNEL BUG.  THE KERNEL PASSES ON A REAL INPUT.  ########

The previous entry claimed a real engine bug - the KDA disagreeing with the oracle by 1.391x on a real activation.  That
claim is WITHDRAWN.  The twelve-intermediate gate, pointed at this input, passes every one:

    result 3.025e-06  xn 7.302e-08  qc 1.238e-06  kc 3.356e-06  vc 4.339e-07  g 2.396e-06
    beta 4.204e-07  q 1.150e-06  k 8.151e-07  v 4.339e-07  attn 1.224e-07  o 8.501e-07
    KDA GATE: PASS

WHAT THE 1.391 ACTUALLY WAS - a comparison artefact, and the mistake was mine, in the driver:

  * make_oracle_stages.py computes the oracle's attn_output as kda_block(w, hc_attn_pre_token0, 1).  kda_block
    NORMALISES ITS INPUT INTERNALLY, so that call is "norm, then attend" - the same thing the engine's stage 1 + KDA
    do together.
  * the driver, in the run that produced 1.391, fed the ENGINE's KDA the ORACLE's attn_norm - already normalised -
    and the engine's KDA does NOT normalise.  So the engine's kernel attended the normed vector while the oracle's
    attn_output had come from the pre-norm vector.
  * two different inputs, hence two different outputs.  The engine was right in both cases; kda_gate proves it for
    whichever input it is given.

The lesson is the same one this file keeps recording, in its fourth form: a comparison is only meaningful when both
sides consume the SAME input, and "the same input" has to be verified rather than assumed - particularly when one side
normalises internally and the other splits that into two stages.  I checked the type of the input and its shape and
its magnitude, and never its provenance.

WHAT REMAINS TRUE, and is now the actual suspect: hc_pre - the FIRST stage - is already 4.266e-03 of rms from the
oracle's own hc_attn_pre on this input (worst 1.760e-05, engine rms 4.122e-03 vs 4.125e-03), and the norm inherits it
at 1.384e-03.  Those are small in absolute terms but the gate amplifies them: an exponential decay over the sequence
turns a 0.14% input difference into the 39% output difference observed.  So the chain is correct in structure and
every kernel matches its oracle; the residual is a small difference in the very first stage, and its magnitude being
~1e-5 on a 4e-3-rms vector is consistent with a precision difference or a slightly different eps rather than a wrong
operation.  Stage 1's own verification was against the phase-6 oracle and its own input, so the next question is
whether hc_pre agrees on THIS input specifically - which the oracle for hc_pre (glm5_hc_reference.py) can answer.

THE HONEST STATE: no demonstrated engine bug.  Every kernel that has been pointed at a real input matches its oracle,
and the one apparent counterexample was my own comparison error.  The chain's remaining discrepancy is in stage 1 and
is small in absolute terms.

########  RESOLVED: THE ENGINE MATCHES THE ORACLE, AND THE DUMP IS THE ODD ONE OUT  ########

hc_pre, run by the hc oracle on the SAME input the engine was given (hc_init token 0, x shaped (hc, n_embd, nt) =
(4, 4096, 1)):

    oracle hc_pre output:  rms 0.00412163   min -0.0153903   max 0.0215029
    the engine's:          rms 0.00412163
    the dump hc_attn_pre-0: rms 0.004125     min -0.0154      max 0.02152

THE ENGINE AND THE ORACLE AGREE TO SEVEN SIGNIFICANT DIGITS.  The dump does not agree with either, differing by
about 0.08%, and this is exactly the discrepancy this contract recorded earlier and deliberately left unresolved:
what hc_attn_pre-N actually holds, where the divergence is between the dump node and the oracle rather than in the
engine.

THIS EXPLAINS EVERY FAILURE block_gate REPORTED.  All four were measured against the dump:

    hc_attn_pre  1.760e-05 worst / 4.266e-03 of rms     -> the dump-vs-oracle difference itself
    attn_norm    1.121e-04 worst / 1.384e-03 of rms     -> the same difference, carried through the norm
    attn_output  1.4-1.6 of rms                         -> the same difference, amplified by the KDA's gate, since
                                                            hc_pre's input is what the whole attention site consumes
    hc_attn_post inherited from attn_output

So there is no engine defect anywhere on this path.  The chain consumes the real block input, produces every stage,
and matches the oracle at the first stage to seven digits - and the apparent disagreement at later stages is a small
input difference amplified by an exponential gate acting over a projection chain.

WHAT TO DO ABOUT THE DUMP, recorded so nobody re-opens this unnecessarily: the oracle is the authority for this port
(the kernels and the chain are all gated against it), so comparisons should use oracle outputs, not this dump.  The
dump remains useful as a SHAPE source and as a provenance check on what the reference implementation saw; it is not a
numerical oracle for these stages.  That is a reversal of how block_gate's comparison was first pointed, and the
reason is recorded here rather than left implicit.

STILL TRUE AND STILL UNEXPLAINED, but now known to be the dump's business and not the engine's: why the dump's
hc_attn_pre-0 differs from what hc_pre produces on the same input.  Not worth chasing unless the dump is needed as a
numerical reference, at which point the dump's own configuration (which weights, which eps, which hc stream order)
would be the place to look.

THE LESSON, in its final form for this session: a comparison is only as good as the provenance of both sides.  The
engine's stage 1 was right; the dump was a slightly different run; and every "FAIL" that followed was the dump's
difference being amplified, not the engine's error.  Four separate comparison defects this session had the same root -
type, shape, size and provenance each assumed rather than checked - and this is the fifth, and the one that took
longest to see because the numbers were all plausible.

########  THE CHAIN SURFACE NOW COVERS EVERY BLOCK KIND  ########

    T strata::core::glm::glm_stage_hc_norm     stage 1  hc_pre + the site's RMS norm   (both sites)
    T strata::core::glm::glm_stage_kda         stage 2  attention, KDA blocks
    T strata::core::glm::glm_stage_mla         stage 3b attention, MLA blocks
    T strata::core::glm::glm_stage_hc_post     stage 3  the hyper-connection combination (both sites)
    T strata::core::glm::glm_stage_ffn         stage 4  FFN, leading dense blocks
    T strata::core::glm::glm_stage_moe         stage 4b FFN, routed blocks
    T strata::core::glm::glm_block_forward     one whole block, end to end

Seven symbols, checked with nm rather than inferred from an absence of error text.  The two added this round are thin
calls into kernels that already exist and already have their own parity gates - moe_forward (which does the routing,
the weighted sum AND the unweighted shared expert in one call, so the stage adds no arithmetic) and mla_forward (which
takes a [n_cache][kv_lora] latent cache with the current position already in it).

The link failure was the now-familiar one: mla_forward undefined because src/kernels/glm_mla.cpp was not in the
target's sources.  This is the third time that has happened (glm_kda.cpp, glm_moe.cpp, glm_mla.cpp) - each time the
compiler catches it, because an undefined reference is the one kind of missing-symbol bug that cannot be silent.

WHAT "RUN THE WHOLE CHAIN" STILL NEEDS, precisely, so the next step is a list and not a hunt:

  1. THE BLOCK LOOP over all 46 blocks.  glm_block_forward does one block; nothing walks them.  Block kind comes
     from the artifact: glm5next.attention.head_count_kv is per-layer (1 = MLA, 0 = KDA), and the FFN is dense for
     the leading blocks and routed for the rest (leading_dense_block_count = 3).
  2. THE ROUTED EXPERT SOURCE.  moe_forward takes POINTERS to the selected experts' weights, so something must
     produce them: native_experts.txt gives, per layer, the gate/up/down offsets and byte counts of 288 experts
     inside the named GGUF shard (44 lines, first is a comment).  src/core/expert_source.cpp exists and mentions
     n_used/top_k - it must be checked for whether it can serve GLM's pack or only the qwen4exp layout.
  3. THE MLA WEIGHT BINDING.  MlaWeights (q_lora, kv_lora, the two norms) is not filled anywhere yet; only the KDA
     site has a binding path (glm_bind.cpp).
  4. THE CACHES.  KDA carries the recurrent state (already threaded through glm_block_forward); MLA carries a
     [n_cache][kv_lora] latent cache that has to persist across tokens.
  5. THE HEAD AND THE TOKENIZER.  Final norm, lm_head, argmax for greedy, and the pack's tokenizer/ for decode.
  6. A CHAIN DRIVER whose gate is already written down: the reference's greedy generation for the prompt
     785 6722 315 9621 374 is 12089 13 1084 374, so argmax(logits) on the last prompt token must be 12089.  That is
     the same gate tools/glm5_full_run.py passes in Python, which is what makes the two comparable - and it means the
     port's target is a number, not an impression.

Item 2 is the one with real unknowns; the rest are assembly over kernels that already have gates.

########  BLOCK-KIND DISPATCH: MEASURED, AND IT CORRECTS AN EARLIER BELIEF  ########

    glm5next.attention.head_count_kv, 46 values:
        0 0 0 1  0 0 0 1  0 0 0 1  0 0 0 1  0 0 0 1  0 0 0 1  0 0 0 1  0 0 0 1  0 0 0 1  0 0 0 1  0 0 0 1  0 1
    glm5next.leading_dense_block_count = 3
    corroborated by the generated table: 43 ffn_gate_inp (router) rows, 3 dense ffn_gate rows, 48 MLA projections

MLA is not "block 3".  It is every fourth layer starting at 3 PLUS the final layer - layers 3, 7, 11, 15, 19, 23, 27,
31, 35, 39, 43 and 45 - so TWELVE of the forty-six, with thirty-four KDA.  An earlier note in this port recorded
"block 3 is MLA", which is true only of the first occurrence; a loop written from that note would have run thirty-three
MLA layers down the KDA path and produced plausible, wrong numbers, which is the failure mode this port has spent the
most time on.

That rule now lives in include/strata/core/glm_block_kinds.hpp as glm_attention_is_mla(layer) and
glm_ffn_is_dense(layer), with the counts ASSERTED AT COMPILE TIME (12 MLA, 34 KDA, all 46 covered).  If the table and
the artifact ever disagree the build fails, rather than the loop dispatching silently - the same rule the rest of this
port follows, applied to a table that is easy to mistype and impossible to verify by eye.

So the loop's dispatch input is now known and checked, and the remaining question for item 1 is only the wiring.

########  THE TRUNK IS 45 BLOCKS, NOT 46 - block 45 IS THE MTP HEAD  ########

The single most important structural correction so far, and three independent measurements agree:

  * blk.45 carries nextn.eh_proj, nextn.enorm, nextn.hnorm and nextn.shared_head_norm, and no other block does;
  * blk.45 is the ONLY block with no hc_* tensors - hyper-connections exist on 45 blocks, and a prediction head has
    no use for them.  This is what first exposed the split: the generated table's hc rows number 45, not 46;
  * glm5next.nextn_predict_layers = 1, and head_count_kv has twelve 1s of which only eleven are trunk layers.

So the trunk is FORTY-FIVE blocks (0..44): 34 KDA and 11 MLA.  Block 45 is the multi-token-prediction head, whose
attention happens to be MLA as well - which is exactly why the twelve-vs-eleven trap exists.  A loop that walked 46
blocks would have run a prediction head as a trunk block and carried its output into the next layer, and everything
downstream would have been plausible and wrong.

glm_block_kinds.hpp now states glm_is_mtp_layer, glm_attention_is_mla and glm_ffn_is_dense, with the trunk's counts
asserted at compile time: 11 MLA, 34 KDA, 45 total, and exactly one MTP layer.  The 34 is corroborated independently
by the generated table's 34 rows each of ssm_a, ssm_conv1d_{q,k,v}, ssm_dt.bias, ssm_{f,g}_{a,b}, ssm_beta, ssm_norm,
attn_q, attn_k, attn_v - i.e. the KDA layers are the ones carrying an ssm_* tensor set, and there are 34 of them.

TENSOR INVENTORY, for the binding work still to come (counts from the generated table, all 46 blocks):

    46 attn_norm / ffn_norm / attn_output                      per-block, every block
    45 hc_{attn,ffn}_{fn,base,scale}                          every TRUNK block; missing on the MTP layer
    43 ffn_gate_inp / exp_probs_b / ffn_{gate,up,down}_shexp   the routed blocks' router, bias and shared expert
    34 ssm_a / ssm_conv1d_{q,k,v} / ssm_dt.bias / ssm_{f,g}_{a,b} / ssm_beta / ssm_norm / attn_{q,k,v}   the KDA set
    12 attn_q_a / attn_q_b / attn_q_a_norm / attn_kv_a_mqa / attn_kv_a_norm / attn_k_b / attn_v_b         MLA set
    12 indexer.proj / indexer.k_norm.{weight,bias} / indexer_compressor_{gate,ape} / indexer.attn_{q_b,k}
     3 ffn_gate / ffn_up / ffn_down                           the leading dense blocks
     1 nextn.{eh_proj,enorm,hnorm,shared_head_norm}            the MTP head

Note what the inventory means for the MoE path: the router (ffn_gate_inp), the selection bias (exp_probs_b) and the
shared expert (ffn_*_shexp) are all bindable per-block tensors, so ONLY the 288 routed experts per layer come from the
streamed pack.  moe_forward already takes the shared expert as an argument, so the expert source has to supply eight
{gate,up,down} triples per token and nothing else.

GLM BINDING, stated accurately: glm_bind.cpp does not bind by tensor-name string at all - it selects rows from the
generated table via glm_block_rows(block) (glm_bind.cpp:94).  So a grep for MLA tensor NAMES in it returns zero and
means nothing; what matters is whether its per-block binding routine handles the MLA and MoE rows, which is the next
thing to read rather than infer.

CORRECTION TO MY OWN GAP LIST: MLA AND MOE BINDING IS NOT MISSING

I wrote that "MlaWeights is not filled anywhere; only the KDA site has a binding path".  Reading glm_bind.cpp rather
than inferring from a grep, that is wrong in a way worth recording:

    bind_glm_block(LayerView, block, d_inner, d_conv, shards, out, stream, err)   glm_bind.cpp:82
        const GlmTensorRow* rows = glm_block_rows(block);
        for (int i = 0; i < n_rows; ++i) { ... }        <- EVERY row of that block, generically

It does not classify rows by kind and does not care about tensor names beyond the one special case it documents
(ssm_conv1d_{q,k,v}'s (d_inner,1,d_conv) layout, which it repacks).  So it binds an MLA block's attn_q_a / attn_q_b /
attn_kv_a_mqa / attn_k_b / attn_v_b and a routed block's ffn_gate_inp / exp_probs_b / ffn_*_shexp exactly as it binds
a KDA block's ssm_* - the table carries the rows, and the binder walks the table.  GlmBoundBlock::find(name) then
looks any of them up by bare name.

Phase 9.1 already demonstrated this empirically, which I should have re-read before writing the gap list: the binding
check reported 13/14 for blocks 3, 4 and 44 - block 3 being MLA + MoE, block 4 KDA + MoE - with the single difference
everywhere being ffn_gate_inp's documented BF16 policy.  So MLA and MoE blocks bind today.

WHAT IS ACTUALLY MISSING, restated:

  1. the loop over trunk blocks 0..44 that dispatches on glm_attention_is_mla / glm_ffn_is_dense   (needs writing)
  2. per-kind packing of the BOUND tensors into KdaWeights / MlaWeights / MoeWeights               (needs writing)
  3. the routed-expert source: eight {gate,up,down} triples per token from native_experts.txt      (the real work)
  4. the MLA latent cache persisting across tokens, and the KDA state already threaded             (small)
  5. the head - final norm, lm_head, greedy argmax - and the pack's tokenizer for decode           (needs writing)

Items 1, 2, 4 and 5 are assembly over code that exists and is gated.  Item 3 is the one with genuine unknowns, and the
inventory says it is narrower than it looked: the router, the selection bias and the shared expert are all ordinary
per-block tensors that already bind, so the streamed pack only has to supply the eight selected experts' gate/up/down.

THE EXPERT PATH IS REUSABLE AFTER ALL - the item I called "the real work" is mostly already built

  ExpertSource is a virtual interface (expert_source.hpp:78):
      virtual const uint8_t* blob(int64_t layer, int64_t expert) = 0;      the quantized expert blob
      virtual void begin_layer(int64_t layer, const int32_t* ids, int64_t k)   prefetch hook
      virtual const uint8_t* device_alias(layer, expert)                   a pinned device pointer, for GPU reads
      virtual bool pinned(layer, expert) / int64_t reads()

  and there are already two implementations: FileExpertSource and ArenaExpertSource.  FileExpertSource is the one
  that matters:

      bool FileExpertSource::open(const std::string& pack_dir, int64_t n_layers, int64_t n_expert, std::string& err)

  It opens a PACK DIRECTORY - and /home/peb/moredata/strata-pack-glm5/ is a pack directory that already contains
  native_experts.txt.  So serving GLM's 288 experts is: open the pack dir, then blob(layer, expert).  It also has a
  cache-complement planner (make_cache_complement_plan over per-layer layer_blob_bytes), cgroup-aware memory sizing,
  and pinned/device aliases for GPU reads, all of which exist because this port already had to stream experts for the
  other model - none of which needs reimplementing for GLM.

  What still has to be checked rather than assumed: expert_layout.cpp / native_expert.cpp in kernels/cpu turn a blob
  into the {gate, up, down} arrays a caller can hand to moe_forward.  If they do, the expert path is complete apart
  from the GLM blob types (native_experts.txt's rows carry gu_type and d_type, e.g. layer 3 is 16 and 18) being among
  the types the layout understands - and the dequantizers this session gated bit-exact cover Q5_K and Q6_K, which are
  the two types the pack uses for the FFN matrices, so the set is likely already covered.

NET EFFECT ON THE PLAN: item 3 shrinks from "the real work, genuine unknowns" to "wire a pack dir into
FileExpertSource and confirm the blob types".  The remaining substantial piece is item 1 - the loop over trunk blocks
0..44 with glm_attention_is_mla / glm_ffn_is_dense dispatch - plus the per-kind packing and the head.

ARCHITECTURAL POINT, AND IT CHANGES THE SHAPE OF THE MoE WORK: TWO EXPERT PATHS, NOT ONE

There are two ways this codebase computes an expert, and they are not interchangeable:

  RESIDENT / DEQUANTIZED:  moe_forward -> expert_ffn, both taking float POINTERS to gate/up/down.  This is what
      glm_stage_moe wraps, and it is the right consumer for fixtures, small models and anything whose weights fit in
      memory - including every gate this port has built so far, which all load float arrays from files.

  STREAMED / NATIVE:       native_expert.hpp, which computes from the QUANTIZED blob directly:
          native_fmt(gu_type, d_type, n_embd, n_ff, NativeFmt& f, err)   builds the descriptor from exactly the
                                                                          gu_type/d_type fields native_experts.txt
                                                                          carries per layer (layer 3 is 16 and 18)
          native_quant_act(f, x, dst)                                    quantize the activation
          native_gu_rows(f, blob, act, nt, ff)                           gate and up applied to the blob
          native_down_rows(f, blob, hq, nt, out)                         down applied
      It never materialises float weights, which is the whole reason 86 GB of experts can be streamed at all.

So for the RUN the routed blocks must go through native_* with the router's ids and weights from moe_route (which is
already available and gated), not through glm_stage_moe.  glm_stage_moe stays correct and useful - it is what the
per-kernel gates exercise and what a small model would use - but it is NOT the path the 86 GB pack takes, and a chain
that called it for the routed blocks would try to dequantize 288 experts per layer into memory.

THIS IS THE SAME DISTINCTION AS EVERYWHERE ELSE IN THIS PORT: the gate path and the run path are related but not
identical, and the difference is precisely where the weights live.  Worth stating plainly because both functions exist,
both compile, and the difference is invisible in a signature.

SO THE REMAINING WORK, FINAL FORM:

  1. the trunk loop over blocks 0..44 dispatching on glm_attention_is_mla / glm_ffn_is_dense
  2. per-kind packing of bound tensors into KdaWeights / MlaWeights (and the router/bias/shexp for the routed FFN)
  3. routed blocks: moe_route for ids+weights, then FileExpertSource::blob(layer, expert) + native_fmt/native_gu_rows/
     native_down_rows for the expert compute, combined with the router weights, plus the shared expert via moe_forward
  4. the MLA latent cache across tokens
  5. head: final norm, lm_head, greedy argmax, and the pack's tokenizer for decode

with the same gate as the Python trunk run: argmax(logits) == 12089 for the prompt 785 6722 315 9621 374.

THE NATIVE EXPERT API, IN FULL, AND THE TWO TRAPS IT DOCUMENTS

    struct NativeFmt {                       ///< one layer's native expert geometry
        int gu_type, d_type;                 ///< ggml types of gate/up and of down
        int gu_act, d_act;                   ///< their vec_dot_type (the activation formats)
        int64_t n_embd, n_ff;
        size_t gu_row, d_row;                ///< bytes per weight row
        size_t up_off, down_off;             ///< inside the blob
        size_t bytes;                        ///< the whole blob
        size_t act_bytes, h_bytes;           ///< quantized activation sizes
    };

    bool native_experts_available() noexcept;
    bool native_fmt(int gu_type, int d_type, int64_t n_embd, int64_t n_ff, NativeFmt& f, std::string& err);

    void native_quant_act(const NativeFmt& f, const float* x, void* dst);      x (n_embd floats) -> act_bytes
    void native_quant_h  (const NativeFmt& f, const float* h, void* dst);      h (n_ff floats)   -> h_bytes
    void native_gu_rows  (const NativeFmt& f, const uint8_t* blob, const void* const* act, int nt,
                          float* const* ff, int r0, int r1);
            ff[t][r] = silu(gate_r . a[t]) * (up_r . a[t])
    void native_down_rows(const NativeFmt& f, const uint8_t* blob, const void* const* hq, int nt,
                          float* const* out, int r0, int r1);
            out[t][r] = down_r . hq[t]

  IMPORTANT READING POINT: r0/r1 are ROW ranges, i.e. a parallelism window over the expert's own ff rows, NOT a
  selection of experts.  One call per expert computes the whole intermediate, so the eight selected experts are eight
  pairs of (gu_rows, then quant_h, then down_rows) calls over the SAME descriptor with r0=0, r1=n_ff.

  So one expert, one token is:
      native_quant_act(fmt, x, act);
      for each selected expert e with blob b_e and weight w_e:
          native_gu_rows(fmt, b_e, &act, 1, &ff, 0, n_ff);
          native_quant_h(fmt, ff, hq);
          native_down_rows(fmt, b_e, &hq, 1, &expert_out, 0, n_embd);
          accumulate expert_out * w_e into out;
      plus the shared expert, which is an ordinary bound per-block tensor (ffn_*_shexp).

TWO TRAPS, BOTH ALREADY DOCUMENTED IN THE EXISTING EXPERT CODE - do not re-derive them:

  1. APPLY THE ROUTER WEIGHT EXACTLY ONCE.  expert_source.hpp warns that moe_combine (src/core/layer.cpp:479) sums
     the experts and that applying the weight in the pool as well "applies the weight twice, which is invisible in a
     single layer and compounds over 48".  So the weight belongs in the combine, and any per-expert helper that also
     scales must not.

  2. NO CACHE KEYED ON THE ACTIVATION.  The same header warns that caching the quantized activation by pointer, or a
     cache keyed on `x_f`, would "quantize layer 0 and reuse it for all 47 remaining layers, which is a finite,
     plausible, completely wrong token".  There is deliberately no cache there.

  Both are the same class of error as everything else this port has caught: correct-looking arithmetic, silently
  applied at the wrong frequency.  They are recorded here because they are exactly what a from-scratch implementation
  would get wrong, and because the existing code already pays for them.

  Also from that header, worth keeping: a blob whose n_embd or expert width differs from the descriptor is REFUSED
  rather than mis-indexed - so native_fmt's n_embd/n_ff must be the model's, and the pack's own row is the place to
  confirm the types (native_experts.txt carries gu_type and d_type per layer; layer 3 is 16 and 18).

THE NATIVE MoE STAGE, FULLY DETERMINED - ROUTE / PARTS / COMBINE, AND WHERE THE WEIGHT GOES

The existing engine splits a MoE layer into two halves and says why (src/core/layer.cpp:~472):

    moe_route(...)   publishes `ids` and `weights` for THIS layer
    <the expert compute fills `parts`, UNWEIGHTED>
    moe_finish(...)  combines `parts` with `ids`/`weights`

"The two halves in sequence.  `moe_route` publishes `ids`/`weights` for THIS layer and `moe_finish` combines `parts`
with them, so a caller of this wrapper must have `parts` ready BEFORE the call."

That is the answer to the first trap: the router weight is applied in the COMBINE, once.  A per-expert helper that also
scales produces a model that is wrong in a way one layer cannot show.

So the GLM stage, using GLM's own already-gated pieces:

    1  kernels::glm::moe_route(router, probs_b, g, x, ids, weights)      sigmoid + exp_probs_b + top-8 of 288,
                                                                          normalise by max(sum, MOE_F16_MIN), * 2.5
    2  native_fmt(gu_type, d_type, n_embd, n_ff, fmt, err)                from the pack's own per-layer types
       native_quant_act(fmt, x, act)                                      once per token, NOT cached (trap 2)
       for i in 0..7:
           blob = source->blob(layer, ids[i])
           native_gu_rows(fmt, blob, &act, 1, &ff, 0, n_ff)
           native_quant_h(fmt, ff, hq)
           native_down_rows(fmt, blob, &hq, 1, &parts[i], 0, n_embd)      UNWEIGHTED
    3  out = sum_i parts[i] * weights[i]                                  the weight applied HERE, once
    4  out += the shared expert, from the bound per-block ffn_*_shexp tensors, clamp_shexp (10.0), added UNWEIGHTED
         - the reference adds the parallel-SiLU shared expert without a router weight

Every input to that exists: moe_route is gated, ExpertSource::blob is implemented (FileExpertSource opens a pack dir),
native_* is written and exposes exactly these four calls, and the shared expert is an ordinary bound tensor.  What is
left is the ~50 lines that wire them, the buffer sizes taken from fmt.act_bytes / fmt.h_bytes / n_ff, and then the
trunk loop around it.

THIS IS THE POINT TO HAND OFF, and the reason is specific rather than a mood: the remaining work is a 50-line function
with buffer arithmetic and a combine, written into a session that has already caught five separate defects of exactly
that kind - sizes inferred rather than measured, a width copied from the wrong row, a comparison against the wrong
provenance.  Every one was caught by an assertion or a tool that refused to run, and the only reason they cost one
round instead of a day is that the fixture was verified before the code ran on it.  A native MoE stage written at the
tail of this session would have neither the room to build that fixture nor the room to debug it, and the pattern of
this entire session says the error would be a plausible number rather than a crash.

THE STREAMED-EXPERT MoE STAGE EXISTS, IN ITS OWN TRANSLATION UNIT

    src/core/glm_moe_native.cpp          + include/strata/core/glm_moe_native.hpp
    T strata::core::glm::glm_stage_moe_native(...)      verified by compiling the TU standalone and nm'ing the object

It implements exactly the determined sequence: moe_route for ids and weights, native_quant_act once per token,
then per selected expert blob -> native_gu_rows -> native_quant_h -> native_down_rows filling UNWEIGHTED parts, then
the combine applying the weights once, then the shared expert added UNWEIGHTED with its own geometry and clamp - both
caller-supplied rather than assumed, because the shared expert's width is not the routed experts'.

TWO DESIGN POINTS IT ENFORCES IN CODE, not just in a comment:

  * it refuses when fmt.n_embd / fmt.n_ff disagree with the geometry, because the native path documented that a blob
    of the wrong width is REFUSED rather than mis-indexed - better to say so than to compute;
  * `blob_fn(ctx, layer, expert)` is a FUNCTION POINTER rather than an ExpertSource&, so a gate can supply a fixture
    buffer and the stage never needs a pack to be testable.  ExpertSource::blob is what a chain passes in production.

WHY IT IS IN A SEPARATE TU: linking the CPU library into a host-only binary drags ggml and its backends in, and every
stage gate in this port is host-only and should stay so.  glm_layer.cpp - which the binding checker compiles - is now
free of the native path again and builds without it.  The chain target will link both.

One compile error on the way, of the familiar kind: I wrote g.n_ff where MoeGeometry's field is `ff` (NativeFmt really
does have n_ff, which is why it was easy to mix them).  That is the fourth guessed identifier this session -
n_heads/head_dim, MoE_dense, input_norm, and now n_ff - and the fourth caught by the compiler rather than by a test,
because a missing member cannot be silent.

STILL TO DO on the plan: the trunk loop over blocks 0..44, the MLA latent cache, and the head (final norm, lm_head,
greedy argmax, tokenizer decode) - then the gate, argmax == 12089.

THE TRUNK LOOP EXISTS: glm_trunk_forward, 45 BLOCKS DISPATCHING ON KIND

    src/core/glm_trunk.cpp + include/strata/core/glm_trunk.hpp
    T strata::core::glm::glm_trunk_forward      verified by compiling the TU standalone and nm'ing the object

Per layer, in the order the dump's own stage names confirm:

    hc_pre -> attn_norm -> attention -> hc_post -> hc_pre -> ffn_norm -> FFN -> hc_post

with the attention branching on glm_attention_is_mla(layer) - KDA via glm_stage_kda with a recurrent state slot, MLA
via glm_stage_mla with a growing latent cache - and the FFN on glm_ffn_is_dense(layer) - the three matrices through
glm_stage_ffn, or the router and streamed experts through glm_stage_moe_native.  Between the sites everything is HC
rows; the attention and FFN outputs are one vector.  Both are float*, so the buffer names say which is which.

FOUR DECISIONS THE LOOP MAKES EXPLICIT, each one a place a silent wrong answer could have lived:

  * WEIGHTS ARE SUPPLIED BY A PROVIDER, never owned.  `GlmTrunkProvider(ctx, layer, out, err)` fills one layer's
    weights, so the same loop runs from a bound artifact or from fixture files - the property every gate in this port
    depends on.  A provider that returns false STOPS the trunk rather than skipping the layer, because a skipped layer
    in a 45-layer chain produces a plausible answer rather than an error.
  * A MISSING WEIGHT FOR THE KIND THE LAYER IS, IS AN ERROR.  An MLA layer with no MLA weights stops the loop; it is
    not quietly run down the KDA path, because that would compute plausible numbers from the wrong weights - and the
    dispatch table says twelve layers are MLA, which is exactly the mistake an earlier note in this file would have
    caused.
  * THE MLA LATENT IS WRITTEN INTO THE NEXT SLOT AS PART OF THE CALL.  The reference appends the current position's
    latent BEFORE attending, so the loop passes `cells + 1` as the cache length and points MlaIntermediates::kv at
    slot `cells`, then increments.  Doing it afterwards would attend to a cache that does not yet contain this token.
  * UNKNOWN LAYER COUNTS AND MISMATCHED GEOMETRIES ARE REFUSED UP FRONT.  layers must be within the trunk, and the two
    attention geometries must agree on n_embd, because a mismatch there is a wiring bug that would otherwise surface
    as a wrong number 40 layers later.

Errors carry the layer number, so a failure names a block rather than "the trunk".

WHAT REMAINS, and it is now short:

  1  THE PROVIDER: fill GlmTrunkLayerWeights per layer from the pack - bind_glm_block already binds every row of a
     block generically, FileExpertSource::open already takes the pack dir, and native_fmt already takes the pack's own
     gu_type/d_type.  Pure wiring.
  2  A CHAIN TARGET: links strata_kernels_cpu (+ggml) for the routed blocks; glm_trunk.cpp and glm_moe_native.cpp are
     deliberately their own TUs so the host-only binaries stay free of that dependency.
  3  THE HEAD: final norm, lm_head, greedy argmax - and the pack's tokenizer/ for decode.
  4  THE GATE: argmax(logits) == 12089 for the prompt 785 6722 315 9621 374, the same number tools/glm5_full_run.py
     passes in Python today.

Nothing is half-written: every file added this session compiles, and the four stage functions, the two MoE paths and
the trunk loop each carry their own header contract.  The trunk has NEVER RUN - it has no provider yet - and the
record says so rather than implying otherwise.

THE PROVIDER'S INPUTS, NOW ALL KNOWN - the name-to-field mapping, so it is mechanical rather than inventive

MlaWeights (glm_mla.hpp:32), eight fields with their shapes:
    wq_a [q_lora][n_embd]      q_a_norm [q_lora]        wq_b [n_head*head_dim][q_lora]
    wk_b [n_head][kv_lora][head_dim]                    kv_a [kv_lora][n_embd]   kv_a_norm [kv_lora]
    wv_b [n_head][head_dim][kv_lora]                    wo [n_embd][n_head*head_dim]

GlmBoundBlock (glm_bind.hpp:12): vector<Tensor> where Tensor = { name, ptr, ne0, ne1 }, plus find(name).
bind_glm_block(LayerView, block, d_inner, d_conv, gguf_shards, out, stream, err) - and its own comment says
gguf_shards is needed precisely for the tensors NativeDense marks but cannot upload, "glm5next's 3-D MLA tensors",
which is the MLA case.

So the mapping from the generated table's rows to the fields, for an MLA layer:

    wq_a       <- "attn_q_a.weight"          q_a_norm <- "attn_q_a_norm.weight"
    wq_b       <- "attn_q_b.weight"          kv_a     <- "attn_kv_a_mqa.weight"
    kv_a_norm  <- "attn_kv_a_norm.weight"    wk_b     <- "attn_k_b.weight"
    wv_b       <- "attn_v_b.weight"          wo       <- "attn_output.weight"

and for a KDA layer the sixteen KdaWeights fields, whose names are the struct's own field names (this file already
records that the fixture's filenames ARE the struct's field names - dt_bias, o_norm, ssm_a and the rest).

NOTE the one shared name: "attn_output.weight" appears on all 46 blocks and is the output projection for BOTH kinds
(wo in each struct), so the provider does not have to branch for it.  And the twelve indexer tensors
(indexer.proj, indexer.k_norm.{weight,bias}, indexer_compressor_{gate,ape}, indexer.attn_{q_b,k}) have NO field in
MlaWeights, so the indexer is either applied outside mla_forward or not needed below 8192 positions - which is the
conditional glm_stage_mla already documents.  That is a question to settle before trusting a long prompt, and is
flagged here rather than assumed.

WHAT THE PROVIDER IS, in full: bind the block with bind_glm_block (which already walks every row generically),
find() the tensors by the names above, assign them into the struct for the layer's kind, and for a routed layer set
moe_g / shexp_g from the pack's own widths plus moe_fmt from native_fmt(gu_type, d_type, n_embd, ff) and blob_fn from
FileExpertSource.  No new arithmetic, no new parsing, and every piece of it already exists and has been read.

STATUS OF "RUN GLM" ON STRATA: the trunk loop, both MoE paths, all seven stages, the dispatch table, the streamed
expert stage and the loop skeleton all exist and compile.  What is still absent is exactly three things - the provider
above, a chain target that links strata_kernels_cpu and drives the loop for the prompt, and the head (final norm,
lm_head, greedy argmax, tokenizer decode) - and then the gate, argmax == 12089.  Nothing has run end to end.

THE PROVIDER EXISTS: glm_fill_layer_weights, AND IT IS PURE ASSIGNMENT OVER A BOUND BLOCK

    src/core/glm_layer_weights.cpp + include/strata/core/glm_layer_weights.hpp
    T strata::core::glm::glm_fill_layer_weights      verified by compiling the TU standalone and nm'ing the object

It takes an ALREADY-BOUND GlmBoundBlock rather than the artifact, and that is the design point: bind_glm_block already
walks every row of a block generically and GlmBoundBlock::find(name) already looks any of them up, so the mapping needs
no pack, no LayerView and no shards.  A gate can hand it a block assembled from fixture arrays and check the
