assignment - the same property every other gate in this port has, and the reason they can run without a model.

It is deliberately pure assignment: no arithmetic, no dequantization, no layout fixups, because the binder already does
those and a second implementation would be a second thing to disagree with.

The tensor names live in one struct at the top of the file, spelled as the generated table spells them, and two of them
do not follow the obvious pattern - `ssm_a` carries no suffix at all, and `ssm_dt.bias` is a bias.  Those are the two
names that cost this port a round earlier, so they are in one place rather than scattered through the function.

TWO GATES THE PROVIDER KEEPS, both about not silently proceeding:

  * a missing tensor is reported BY NAME ("no tensor attn_kv_a_mqa.weight in the bound block"), because the trunk's own
    check can only say "a required weight is null" - which says nothing about which one or why;
  * the MLA layers HAVE indexer tensors and MlaWeights has no field for them, so the filler refuses when it finds them
    unless kGlmAppliesIndexer is true - which it is not.  The reference's own note says the selection is all-tokens
    below 8192 positions, which is where this model's prompts sit, so dropping them is right here and wrong later; the
    constant and the refusal are what make that a decision rather than an oversight.

k.attn_norm = out.attn_norm for a KDA layer, because the KDA kernel norms its own input with the layer's attn_norm -
the same tensor, reached by two names, and the one place this function has to know that.

FINAL STATE OF THE PORT THIS SESSION: all seven stages, both MoE paths, the trunk loop with kind dispatch, the
compile-time-asserted dispatch table, and the weight provider all exist, compile, and carry verified symbols.  What
remains is one chain target - bind the 45 blocks, open FileExpertSource on the pack dir, set the routed geometries and
the native descriptor from the pack's own types, call glm_trunk_forward, then the head: final norm, lm_head, greedy
argmax, tokenizer decode - and then the gate, argmax == 12089, the same number the Python full run passes.

NOTHING HAS RUN END TO END, and that remains the honest summary: every piece is compiled and symbol-checked, none of
them has been executed together, and the first execution will be the first time the assembly is tested.

THE CHAIN TARGET'S SETUP, RESOLVED - every line of it read from code that already runs

LayerView is not a struct (layout.hpp:72): it is `LayerView(const WeightTable& table, int64_t layer)` with
`name(suffix)` building "blk.<layer>.<suffix>" and `get(suffix)` returning the WeightRef.  And tools/glm5_bind_check.cpp
already does the whole setup, so the chain target does not have to invent any of it:

    WeightTable table;
    table.pool_bytes(pack, bytes, err, &served);          // what will be resident, and how much
    cudaMalloc(&arena, bytes);
    table.load(pack, arena, bytes, err, &served);
    NativeDense nd; nd.load(shards, table, err);
    LayerView v(table, block);
    GlmBoundBlock bound;
    bind_glm_block(v, block, d_inner, d_conv, shards, bound, stream, err);
        with d_inner = 8192, d_conv = 4, "from the artifact's own metadata"

So the chain target is:

    1  build the table, arena, NativeDense once
    2  for layer in 0..44: bind the block, glm_fill_layer_weights(...), and keep the per-layer KdaWeights /
       MlaWeights / shexp-triple STORAGE alive (a vector of them), since GlmTrunkLayerWeights holds pointers
    3  open FileExpertSource on /home/peb/moredata/strata-pack-glm5, and for each routed layer call
       native_fmt(gu_type, d_type, 4096, 2048) with the types native_experts.txt carries for that layer, then set
       moe_g / shexp_g / moe_fmt / blob_fn on the layer's weights
    4  allocate GlmTrunkState: one nh*hd*hd KDA state per KDA layer (zeroed), one [max_cells][kv_lora] MLA cache per
       MLA layer, and the two index maps from glm_block_kinds
    5  seed x from the embedding's streams and call glm_trunk_forward(x, 45, provider, ctx, kda_g, mla_g, 1e-5f,
       state, l_out, stream, err)
    6  the head: final norm, lm_head, greedy argmax, and the pack's tokenizer/ for decode

Then the gate: argmax(logits) == 12089 for 785 6722 315 9621 374 - the same number tools/glm5_full_run.py passes.

Nothing in that list is unknown any more.  Every function, struct, name and recipe has been read rather than inferred,
and the pieces that are new this session have each been compiled and symbol-checked independently:

    glm_stage_hc_norm  glm_stage_kda  glm_stage_mla  glm_stage_hc_post  glm_stage_ffn  glm_stage_moe (resident)
    glm_stage_moe_native (streamed)     glm_block_forward     glm_trunk_forward     glm_fill_layer_weights
    glm_block_kinds.hpp (asserted dispatch)

WHAT HAS NOT HAPPENED: none of it has run together, and the trunk has never executed at all.  The remaining work is
integration of verified parts, and the first execution is the first test of the assembly - which this port has learned,
repeatedly, is a different thing from the sum of its parts passing their own gates.

layer_weights_gate: THE PROVIDER'S MAPPING IS VERIFIED AT RUNTIME, 25 CHECKS PASS

    tools/layer_weights_gate.cpp + a CMake target, run against the fixtures already on disk
    ./build-glm5/layer_weights_gate /home/peb/moredata/glm5-block-l0 /home/peb/moredata/glm5-kda-l0-t1   -> PASS

This is the FIRST runtime verification of any of this session's new chain code: everything else new - the two MoE
paths, the trunk loop, the provider - had only been compiled and symbol-checked.

It builds a GlmBoundBlock from fixture arrays (no pack, no expert source, no bound artifact) and asserts:

  * all fifteen positive mappings, field by field: hc_attn_fn/base/scale, attn_norm, hc_ffn_fn, ffn_norm, and the KDA
    set - wq, wk, ssm_a, dt_bias, ssm_f_a, ssm_f_b, o_norm, wo, attn_norm;
  * the FIVE SWAPS THAT WOULD STILL "WORK", asserted not to hold: f_a against f_b's buffer, f_b against f_a's,
    o_norm against attn_norm's, dt_bias against ssm_a's, ssm_a against dt_bias's.  These are the assertion that earns
    the gate: every one of those mistakes yields a valid float* to a similarly-shaped array and a model that computes
    something.  This port has already been bitten by exactly this class once - the size table where ssm_f_a was given
    ssm_f_b's row - which is why the mapping got its own gate instead of being trusted because it compiled;
  * the ONE TENSOR REACHED BY TWO NAMES: kda.attn_norm is the same pointer as w.attn_norm;
  * a block with one tensor REMOVED fails, and the error names that tensor - checked by dropping ssm_f_b.weight and
    requiring the message to mention it.

A MID-COURSE CORRECTION WORTH RECORDING: the first run reported PASS while the mapping assertions had all been
SKIPPED, because the dense FFN matrices are not in that fixture and the fill returns false before reaching them.  Two
checks had passed and twenty-three had not run - a green result that tested almost nothing, which is the same shape as
the earlier "no error output read as success".  The fix was to recognise that the provider assigns the common and
attention fields BEFORE the FFN step, so a failure there does not invalidate them: the mapping checks now run
regardless, and the failure is pinned to the dense FFN step by name.  A gate that reports PASS because it skipped
everything is worse than no gate, because it produces confidence.

Also corrected in the same pass: I had written `strata::core::kernels::glm::` for the weight structs, but the
namespace is `strata::kernels::glm::` - caught by the compiler, which is the fifth guessed identifier this session.

THE FIRST EXECUTION OF glm_trunk_forward IS FEASIBLE WITHOUT THE PACK - the recipe, measured

Measured just now: /home/peb/moredata/glm5-ffn-l0 holds ONLY raw quantized blobs -
    raw_gate.bin 34,603,008 B   raw_up.bin 34,603,008 B   raw_down.bin 41,287,680 B
plus input.bin / result.bin / engine_output.bin, each 16,384 B (4096 floats).  There are NO float dense matrices on
disk.  And tools/ffn_gate.cpp shows how that is not an obstacle: it dequantizes the raw blobs itself -

    dequant_file(dir + "/raw_gate.bin", 176, ff * n_embd, wg);     // 176-byte blocks -> Q5_K
    dequant_file(dir + "/raw_up.bin",   176, ff * n_embd, wu);
                                            // raw_down.bin is 210-byte blocks -> Q6_K
    if (block_bytes == 176)      dequant_q5_K(...);
    else if (block_bytes == 210) dequant_q6_K(...);

so a trunk gate for ONE layer can build every weight layer 0 needs with no pack, no arena, no expert source:

    hc_attn_*, attn_norm, hc_ffn_*, ffn_norm    <- glm5-block-l0/*.bin (float, already verified loadable)
    the sixteen KDA tensors                     <- glm5-kda-l0-t1/w_*.bin (float, the binding check's own fixture)
    ffn_gate / ffn_up / ffn_down                <- DEQUANTIZED from glm5-ffn-l0/raw_{gate,up,down}.bin

and drive it with the provider this session just wrote and just gated:

    glm_trunk_forward(x, 1, provider_from_fixtures, ctx, kda_g, mla_g, 1e-5f, state, l_out, nullptr, err)

with x = the four HC streams of token 0.

TWO THINGS THAT MAKE IT WORTH DOING rather than merely possible:

  * it is the FIRST EXECUTION of the trunk loop - the assembly of hc_pre, attn_norm, KDA, hc_post, hc_pre, ffn_norm,
    the dense FFN and hc_post in the order the dump's stage names give.  Every one of those stages has passed its own
    gate; none has ever run in sequence;
  * it exercises dequant_q6_K on real tensor data.  dequant_q5_K is gated bit-exact against the oracle's own
    dequantization over 33,554,432 elements, but Q6_K was transcribed and is only reached through this path - so a
    first trunk run is also the first time the transcribed Q6_K touches blk.0.ffn_down.weight, which is exactly
    41,287,680 bytes = 196,608 blocks x 210 B.

WHAT THE COMPARISON IS, and its honest limit: the dump holds l_out-0, the block's own output, so the gate can compare
against it - but this file already records that the DUMP IS THE OUTLIER at hc_pre (rms 0.004125 against the engine's
and the oracle's 0.00412163, about 0.08%), and that the KDA's exponential gate AMPLIFIES exactly that difference.  So
a l_out-0 comparison must be reported as a measured ratio with that amplification named, not as a tight pass/fail -
or, better, the expected output should come from the oracle (glm5_hc_reference.py and glm5_kda_reference.py both exist
and both run on this input already).  A tight tolerance against the dump would produce a FAIL that says nothing about
the trunk, which is the mistake this file has already made once and retracted.

THE TRUNK LOOP HAS NOW EXECUTED.  tools/trunk_gate, block 0, real weights, no pack.

    ./build-glm5/trunk_gate glm5-block-l0 glm5-kda-l0-t1 glm5-ffn-l0 glm5-ref-dump5

    dequantized raw_gate.bin  196608 blocks x 176 B -> 50,331,648 floats   (Q5_K, on device)
    dequantized raw_up.bin    196608 blocks x 176 B -> 50,331,648 floats   (Q5_K, on device)
    dequantized raw_down.bin  196608 blocks x 210 B -> 50,331,648 floats   (Q6_K, on device)
    hc_init ne=[4096,4,5,1] 81920 floats;  l_out-0 ne=[4096,4,5,1] 81920 floats
    engine l_out: rms 0.0058151   sum|.| 75.2591   (loop returned true)
    dump   l_out: rms 0.00599831
    worst absolute difference 0.0121585   ratio engine/dump rms 0.969458
    TRUNK GATE: PASS

What that is: hc_pre -> attn_norm -> KDA -> hc_post -> hc_pre -> ffn_norm -> dense FFN -> hc_post, in this order, on
real weights, with the hyper-connection residual routing between the sites.  Every one of those stages had passed its
own gate in isolation; none of them had ever run in sequence.  That was the whole reason for the tool, and it is now
the first thing in this port that has executed as an assembly rather than as a part.

The three ways an assembly can be wrong that no single stage can be are all checked and all clean: the loop returned
true, the output is finite (not NaN, not inf), and it is not identically zero (sum|.| 75.2591 over 16,384 elements).

THE COMPARISON IS REPORTED, NOT ASSERTED, and the number is worth reading rather than passing: the engine's block
output is 3.1% BELOW the dump's rms (ratio 0.969).  That is consistent with this port's established finding rather
than in tension with it - the dump is the OUTLIER at the block input (its hc_attn_pre is ~0.08% off while the engine
and the oracle agree to seven significant digits), and the KDA's exponential gate amplifies exactly that difference.
A 3% difference at the block output from a 0.08% difference at the input is the amplification this file already
predicted, so the honest reading is that the assembly is broadly right rather than precisely verified.  PRECISE
VERIFICATION NEEDS THE ORACLE'S l_out, not the dump's - glm5_hc_reference.py and glm5_kda_reference.py both already run
on this input, and chaining them to produce block 0's expected output is the next gate.

TWO MISTAKES THE TOOL CAUGHT, both in my own code and both of the class this port keeps having to relearn:

  * SIX SIZE EXPECTATIONS WERE WRONG.  I had assumed hc_attn_fn was 4 streams x 4096 and hc_attn_base was 4 values;
    they are 393,216 floats (24 x 16,384) and 24 values, exactly as the hc contract in this file says - fn (24,16384),
    base (24,).  The loader I wrote trusts the FILE'S OWN LENGTH and treats my expectation as advisory and reported,
    so all six were printed as NOTEs and the engine was given the tensors the files actually hold.  That is the third
    time this session a size I inferred from geometry rather than measured was wrong, and the first time the file
    itself was the authority instead of me;
  * THE DEQUANTIZERS ARE DEVICE KERNELS.  The first run died with "dequant_q5_K launch: an illegal memory access was
    encountered" because I handed them host pointers.  Caught by reading how ffn_gate.cpp does it - cudaMalloc,
    cudaMemcpy to device, kernel, cudaMemcpy back, free - and now copied exactly, including its geometry refusal
    (bytes / block_bytes * 256 != n_elems).

Also: I wrote strata::core::kernels::glm:: for the weight structs AGAIN, in a new file, having fixed the identical
mistake in layer_weights_gate.cpp one commit earlier.  Same fix, same assert.  It is the sixth time this session a
guessed identifier has been caught by the compiler rather than by a test.

WHAT REMAINS, unchanged in shape and now much smaller: the pack provider (routed blocks, FileExpertSource, native_fmt
from the pack's own gu_type/d_type), the loop over all 45 blocks with KDA states and MLA caches, the head, and the
token gate argmax == 12089.  The assembly is proven to execute; what is unproven is that it is numerically right, and
that is what the oracle's block output would settle.

CORRECTION AND A LIVE DISCREPANCY: the engine's l_out is 3.0% BELOW BOTH references

The oracle's own block-0 output was produced with the runner's existing dump path, no new code:

    $PY tools/glm5_full_run.py --gguf $GGUF --tokens "785 6722 315 9621 374" --blocks 1 \
        --dump /home/peb/moredata/glm5-oracle-lout
    -> wrote l_out-0.bin (327,700 B = 5-word header + 81,920 floats)
    -> "block 0 (KDA, dense) done; |l_out| 1.7166"

and the same trunk_gate run, pointed at that directory instead of the ref dump:

    oracle l_out-0 rms   0.00599758
    dump   l_out-0 rms   0.00599831        <- the oracle and the dump AGREE, 0.012% apart
    engine l_out   rms   0.0058151         <- 3.0% below both (ratio 0.96958)
    worst absolute difference 0.0427237

THIS RETRACTS THE EXPLANATION I WROTE TWENTY MINUTES AGO, and the retraction matters more than the result.  After the
first run I recorded that the 3.1% gap was "consistent with this port's established finding that the dump is the
outlier at hc_pre, amplified by the KDA's exponential gate".  That reasoning is now dead: at l_out the ORACLE and the
DUMP agree to 0.012%, so the dump cannot be the thing the engine is disagreeing with.  The engine is 3% below a
reference that two independent sources agree on.  The note still printed by trunk_gate ("the dump is this port's known
outlier...") is therefore MISLEADING AT THIS STAGE and needs rewording rather than keeping, because it would let a
future reader excuse a real 3% gap as an artifact.

WHERE THE 3% CAN STILL LEGITIMATELY COME FROM, and it is one specific thing that has not been excluded:

    THE ENGINE DID NOT CONSUME THE SAME INPUT THE ORACLE DID.

trunk_gate feeds x from glm5-ref-dump5/hc_init.bin - the REFERENCE DUMP's embedding streams - while the oracle
computed its block-0 input itself from the token ids.  This file already records that those two differ: at
hc_attn_pre the dump reads rms 0.004125 while the engine AND the oracle both read 0.00412163, about 0.08%.  A 0.08%
difference at the input, through a KDA whose gate is exponential and which the measurement above shows produces a
280x amplification at the attention output, is exactly the size that lands as ~3% at l_out.

So the comparison is NOT YET APPLES TO APPLES, and it cannot distinguish "the assembly is 3% wrong" from "the assembly
was given a 0.08%-different input".  That is the single thing to fix next:

    1  have the oracle dump ITS OWN block-0 input streams (the hc_pre input it computes), i.e. one more write_tensor
       in the runner's dump path - the same file the engine already reads, so trunk_gate needs no change;
    2  re-run with the engine consuming the oracle's input;
    3  THEN the tolerance can be tight.  If the engine still sits 3% off, that is a real assembly bug and the hunt
       starts inside the block; if it collapses to the fp32 floor, the assembly is verified and the 3% was the input.

Nothing about the 3% should be called either way until step 3 - which is precisely the mistake that was just made and
retracted above.

ALSO WORTH RECORDING so it does not mislead later: the 1-block oracle run prints "logits argmax = 134277 expected
12089 MISMATCH".  That is EXPECTED and meaningless here - --blocks 1 runs only block 0, so the head is being applied
to a single-block hidden state.  The 12089 gate only means anything for a full 45-block run.

THE BLOCK COMPARISON HAS NEVER BEEN APPLES-TO-APPLES - the two hc_init files hold the SAME VALUES IN A DIFFERENT ORDER

With the oracle's own input written (tools/glm5_full_run.py now dumps hc_init as well as l_out-N), the same trunk_gate
run gives a very different result:

    engine, fed the REF DUMP's hc_init : l_out rms 0.0058151    vs oracle 0.00599758   ratio 0.970  (3% low)
    engine, fed the ORACLE's  hc_init : l_out rms 0.00828296   vs oracle 0.00599758   ratio 1.381  (38% high)
    oracle l_out-0 rms 0.00599758   ref dump l_out-0 rms 0.00599831   (oracle and dump agree to 0.012%)

A 42% swing in the engine's output from swapping input files is not a numerical sensitivity story, so the two files were
compared directly:

    ref-dump hc_init : ne=[4096,4,5,1]  n=81920  rms 0.00827007  min -0.0413961  max 0.0407766
    oracle   hc_init : ne=[4096,4,5,1]  n=81920  rms 0.00827007  min -0.0413961  max 0.0407766
    elementwise      : worst 0.0617329   rel-to-rms 7.46   mean|.| 0.00893637   identical elements 17 of 81920

IDENTICAL STATISTICS TO SIX DIGITS, 17 OF 81,920 ELEMENTS THE SAME.  The two files hold the SAME MULTISET OF VALUES
IN A DIFFERENT ARRANGEMENT - confirmed by np.sort being equal - and not a simple axis transpose: brute-forcing the
permutation over the (4096,4,5) reshaping leaves only the identity shape-compatible, and it is off by 0.0617.  So the
layout difference is real, is not the obvious one, and has not been identified yet.

WHAT THIS MEANS, and it is the important part:

  * THE ENGINE'S INPUT IS A LAYOUT ASSUMPTION, AND THE ASSUMPTION IS WHAT IS WRONG.  trunk_gate takes x as the first
    HC*N_EMBD = 16,384 floats of hc_init, on the reasoning that ne0=4096 is fastest so token 0's four streams come
    first.  Both files DO carry ne=[4096,4,5,1], but their first 16,384 floats are different sets of values, so the
    slice the engine consumed was never the tensor the oracle computed with.  The 3% and the 38% are therefore both
    comparisons of different inputs, and NEITHER NUMBER SAYS ANYTHING ABOUT THE ENGINE'S ARITHMETIC - not the 3% low
    and not the 38% high.  The 3% figure recorded in the previous entry must not be carried forward as evidence of
    anything;
  * This also revisits the earlier "0.08% at hc_attn_pre" explanation: the dump reading 0.004125 against the engine's
    and oracle's 0.00412163 was attributed to a small numerical difference in the input.  Given that the inputs differ
    by a permutation, the rms AGREEING to 0.08% is a coincidence of multiset-preservation, not evidence that the two
    inputs were nearly the same.  An rms is permutation-invariant; it could never have detected this, which is exactly
    why it went unnoticed for a whole session across four separate comparisons.

THE NEXT MEASUREMENT, and it must come before any more block comparison: identify the actual index mapping between the
two hc_init files.  The tools are cheap - the files are 81,920 floats each and the mapping is a permutation, so a
per-value search finds it immediately: for each element of the oracle's file, locate its value in the reference dump's,
and read off the index map.  Then either (a) the engine's slice-and-shape rule is corrected to match, or (b) it is
established which file is in which order and the comparison is run on one of them consistently.

UNTIL THAT IS DONE, no statement about the engine's block arithmetic is supported by evidence.  This port has spent the
session comparing numbers that came from different arrangements of the same data, and the honest position is that the
block-level comparison has not yet been performed.

FOUND THE REAL BUG: write_tensor WROTE C ORDER WHILE ITS HEADER CLAIMED ne0-FASTEST

tools/glm5_block_driver.py:84 promised "data with ne0 fastest" and packed ne = list(a.shape) into the header, then
wrote `a.tobytes()` - which is C order, i.e. the LAST axis fastest.  For a 1-D array the two orders coincide, which is
why it survived; for the 3-D tensors this port is now dumping (4096, 4, 5) they do not, so every file the oracle wrote
had its bytes in one arrangement and its header claiming another.  Fixed to np.asfortranarray + tobytes(order="F").

HOW IT WAS FOUND, and it was not by looking at the writer:

    ref-dump hc_init : 4096-stride copies True   distinct 4096-blocks 5  at [0,4,8,12,16]
    oracle   hc_init : 4096-stride copies False  distinct 4096-blocks 20 at [0..19]

The tensor is the embedding replicated over 4 streams, so in the dump's ne0-fastest layout the four streams are
contiguous 4096-stride copies of each other.  The oracle's file showed NO such copies at any stride - impossible for
that tensor under that header, and enough to convict the writer.

WHY IT COST A WHOLE SESSION OF CONFUSION, and the general lesson:

  * AN RMS IS PERMUTATION-INVARIANT.  Every comparison this port ran used rms, and a permutation leaves rms unchanged.
    So the oracle's l_out-0 "agreed" with the reference dump's to 0.012% (0.00599758 vs 0.00599831) - two files in
    different arrangements, agreeing by construction.  That single fact fabricated the "the oracle and the dump agree,
    therefore the engine is wrong" conclusion, which is now retracted;
  * a per-value index map CANNOT recover the permutation when the data is redundant: hc_init holds only 8,154 unique
    values among 81,920, so every lookup was ambiguous and the "map" I recovered was the first-occurrence artifact
    (it printed oracle->ref [0, 16384, 32768, 49152, 65536, 0, ...], which is meaningless);
  * what DID work was structural: find the invariance the tensor must satisfy (identical streams at a fixed stride)
    and test whether the file satisfies it.  That is what identified the layout, and it needed no reference at all.

THE FIXED COMPARISON, and the real result:

    engine l_out rms 0.0058151   oracle l_out-0 rms 0.00599758
    worst absolute difference 0.0121951   ratio 0.969575     (3.0% low)

and it is the SAME number the engine produced from the reference dump's hc_init (ratio 0.969458, worst 0.0121585) -
which is the consistency check that shows the fix is right: once both files carry ne0-fastest bytes, feeding the engine
either one gives the same answer, as it must, because they hold the same tensor.

SO THE BLOCK COMPARISON HAS NOW ACTUALLY BEEN PERFORMED, for the first time, and the engine is 3.0% BELOW the oracle.
This is a real discrepancy and not an input artifact.

THE LEADING SUSPECT, and it is a specific one that has never been gated: dequant_q6_K.  The engine's dense ffn_down
comes from raw_down.bin's 210-byte blocks, dequantized by dequant_q6_K - transcribed this session, explicitly recorded
as UNWIRED until now, and reaching real tensor data for the FIRST TIME in this very run.  Its sibling dequant_q5_K is
gated bit-exact against the oracle's own dequantization over 33,554,432 elements; Q6_K has never been compared to
anything.  A subtly wrong Q6_K would leave the gate/up path exact, perturb ffn_down, and land as a few percent at
l_out - exactly the shape of what is measured.  The next gate is therefore q6k_parity: dequantize
blk.0.ffn_down.weight's raw blocks and compare against the oracle's own dequantization of the same tensor, the way
q5k_parity does it and NOT by comparing the implementation against itself.

dequant_q6_K IS BIT-EXACT - THE LEADING SUSPECT IS ELIMINATED

    ./build-glm5/q5k_parity /home/peb/moredata/glm5-q6k-parity 210 raw_q5k_blocks.bin
    q5k parity: 196608 raw blocks (41287680 bytes) -> 50331648 elements
      engine : min -0.117431641  max +0.117431641  sum -185.914256
    GATE vs oracle_dequantised.bin: worst 0   relative to rms 0   differing elements 0 of 50,331,648

Zero differing elements over 50,331,648 - the same standard dequant_q5_K met over 33,554,432.  The kernel over the
210-byte blocks of blk.0.ffn_down.weight reproduces the reference exactly, so a wrong Q6_K CANNOT be the cause of the
3.0% the block gate measures at l_out.  The hypothesis recorded in the previous entry was reasonable and is now dead,
which is the point of having a gate for a transcription rather than trusting it.

TWO RECORD CORRECTIONS this exercise turned up, both about my own bookkeeping:

  * I had recorded dequant_q6_K as "transcribed, UNWIRED, never compared against anything".  That was WRONG.  The
    directory /home/peb/moredata/glm5-q6k-parity already held a fixture from 18:25 today - raw_q6k_blocks.bin at
    41,287,680 bytes, exactly 196,608 x 210, i.e. blk.0.ffn_down's blocks - alongside engine_output.bin and
    oracle_dequantised.bin, each 201,326,592 bytes = 50,331,648 floats.  A Q6_K parity fixture with the right tensor,
    its raw blocks and a reference already existed and had already been compared (both sides matched before this run
    too).  So the gate existed; what was missing was the record of it, and the corrected statement is that
    dequant_q6_K is gated bit-exact, not ungated;
  * the Q5_K/Q6_K comparison tool takes its block size and input name as arguments - 176 or 210, and any file name -
    precisely so the second K-quant would not need a second copy of the file.  That was already true before this
    session; it is what let the Q6_K gate be re-run here in one command instead of being rewritten.

WHERE THE 3.0% THEN COMES FROM, having eliminated this: not dequant_q6_K (bit-exact), not dequant_q5_K (bit-exact),
not the KDA kernel or hc_pre/hc_post or the leading dense FFN (each gated independently at 1e-6 to 1e-9 on their own
fixtures).  What has never been compared is the PER-STAGE SEQUENCE inside a block - and that is now the only thing the
3% can be hiding in, because every part is right and the assembly is what has not been checked stage by stage.  The
reference dump holds exactly the per-stage tensors a bisection needs (hc_attn_pre, attn_norm, attn_output,
hc_attn_post, hc_ffn_pre, ffn_norm, ffn_out, l_out), so the next gate is a block bisection: dump the engine's own
intermediates at each of those points and compare them one at a time, in order, until the first one that disagrees -
which localises the 3% to a single line of the assembly instead of leaving it as a block-level number.

One caveat to carry into that: the reference dump's own hc_init and the oracle's now agree only AFTER the writer fix,
and the dump's per-stage tensors were written by the reference dumper (ne0-fastest, correct) - so the bisection can
use the dump's stages directly, but its INPUT must be the oracle's hc_init, not the dump's, until the two are shown
identical elementwise rather than merely equal in rms.  That is the same trap, and it is avoidable by construction now.

THE 3% IS SOLVED: THE KDA WAS BEING NORMALISED TWICE.  BLOCK 0 NOW MATCHES THE ORACLE TO THE FP32 FLOOR.

    before the fix:  engine l_out rms 0.0058151   worst absolute difference 0.0121951   ratio 0.969575
    after  the fix:  engine l_out rms 0.00595305  worst absolute difference 7.35745e-08  ratio 0.992576

THE WORST ELEMENTWISE DIFFERENCE FELL BY 165,000x - from 1.2e-2 to 7.4e-8, which against an rms of ~0.006 is a relative
error of 1.2e-5, i.e. the fp32 noise floor this port already measured (1.480e-05 on the block-1 FFN gate).  Block 0 now
agrees with the oracle to the precision of the arithmetic, and the 3% was never an approximation: it was a bug.

THE BUG, in my own assembly and in one line of glm_trunk_forward:

    src/kernels/glm_kda.cpp:58   `xn[t*ne+i] = xt[i] * inv * w.attn_norm[i]`
        -> the KDA kernel norms its own input internally with attn_norm
    glm5_full_run.py:105,122     `cur_raw = cur  # pre-norm residual`  then  `kda_block(kw, cur_raw.T, ...)`
        -> and the oracle's runner therefore hands it the PRE-norm residual, using `cur = rms_norm(cur, attn_norm)`
           only for MLA
    glm_trunk.cpp (mine)         `glm_stage_hc_norm(cur, ..., w.attn_norm, xn.data(), ...)` and then `glm_stage_kda(xn)`
        -> so the KDA received an ALREADY-normed tensor and normalised it a second time

The fix is `is_mla ? w.attn_norm : nullptr`: the site norm is applied for MLA, and for KDA the norm weight is left to
the kernel that insists on doing it itself.

WHY NO EXISTING GATE COULD HAVE CAUGHT IT, and this is the lesson worth keeping:

  * the KDA's own gate passes 12/12 at ~1e-6 because ITS FIXTURE FEEDS IT A RAW INPUT - the oracle's own writer
    produced that fixture, and the oracle feeds a raw input, so the fixture and the kernel agreed with each other
    while the assembly around them did not;
  * every other stage was likewise correct in isolation: hc_pre to 7 digits, hc_post to 2.6e-7, the dense FFN at the
    fp32 floor, both dequantizers bit-exact.  A test that verifies a part cannot see a wiring error;
  * it took an assembly test - the trunk loop run end to end on real weights and compared against the oracle - to
    reduce the error to one line.  And it took finding the writer bug first, because until the input files were
    ne0-fastest on both sides the comparison could not distinguish this from a dozen other explanations.

IT ALSO RETIRES THE OLD 0.08% STORY AND THE "DUMP IS THE OUTLIER" STORY, both of which were explanations of symptoms
of this bug.  The dump never was the outlier in a way that mattered: what was wrong was the wiring, and every
intermediate number that looked slightly off was downstream of a KDA fed the wrong input.

ONE REPORTING ARTIFACT TO NOTE so the next reader is not misled: the printed ratio (0.992576) is NOT a 0.7% error.
trunk_gate computes the engine's rms over the token slice it ran (16,384 floats) while it computes the dump's rms over
the whole file (81,920 floats, five tokens), so the two rms values have different denominators.  The elementwise
comparison is computed over the common extent and is the meaningful number.  That should be fixed in the tool - rms
over the same extent as the comparison - because a reader who trusts the ratio over the worst-difference would draw
exactly the wrong conclusion.

STATE: the trunk loop executes end to end and block 0 matches the oracle to the fp32 floor.  What remains is the pack
provider for the routed blocks, all 45 blocks with KDA states and MLA caches, the head, and the token gate
argmax == 12089.

THE TOOL NOW REPORTS WHAT IT ACTUALLY MEASURES

    engine l_out: rms 0.00595305
    oracle l_out (same extent): rms 0.00595305
    worst absolute difference 7.35745e-08   relative to rms 1.23591e-05   ratio 1

The reference rms is now taken over the same extent as the comparison, so the ratio reads 1 instead of the 0.992576 the
mismatched denominators produced, and the number beside the worst difference is the one that means something: 1.24e-05
of rms - the fp32 floor this port measured independently on the block-1 FFN gate (1.480e-05).

The retired NOTE is replaced rather than deleted.  It matters that the record shows the old text claimed the dump was
the outlier and that no assertion was possible, and that both claims were symptoms of the double-norm bug: a future
reader who finds only the corrected text will not know that this exact observation was used to excuse a real 3% error
for most of a session.

BLOCK 0 IS VERIFIED.  The assembly executes end to end and agrees with the oracle to 1.2e-05 of rms, which for fp32
accumulation over a KDA block plus a 12288-wide SwiGLU with two dequantized operands is the arithmetic's own floor.

TWO BLOCKS VERIFIED, INCLUDING THE CROSS-BLOCK CHAIN

    block 0 (re-check)        worst 7.35745e-08   relative 1.23591e-05   ratio 1
    block 1 ISOLATED          worst 9.68575e-08   relative 1.33529e-05   ratio 1    fed the ORACLE's l_out-0
    block 1 CHAINED           worst 1.16415e-07   relative 1.60491e-05   ratio 1    fed the ENGINE's l_out-0

Three things ran for the first time and all three are at the fp32 floor:

  * a SECOND block, with its own real weights (built from the artifact by tools/make_block_fixture.py 1, which already
    took the block number as an argument - no new code);
  * the CROSS-BLOCK CHAIN: block 0's output became block 1's input.  The chained error (1.16e-07) is barely above the
    isolated one (9.69e-08), which is the point - the engine's own block-0 output is as good an input as the oracle's,
    exactly as block 0's own 7.4e-08 agreement predicts;
  * the error grows monotonically with depth (7.36e-08, 9.69e-08, 1.16e-07) as fp32 accumulation should, rather than
    jumping by an order of magnitude, which is what a wiring error looks like.

HOW, WITHOUT THE BIG REFACTOR: layer 1 is ALSO KDA + dense and its tensor names are identical to layer 0's, so the
loop's kind dispatch is the same for both.  trunk_gate therefore needed only an input-file name and an output-file
writer to chain two runs by running the tool twice - the second consuming the first's output.  The `layer` argument
selects which block's weights the provider serves.  This is NOT a substitute for the 45-block loop, and it is recorded
as what it is: a way to execute and verify a second block before the pack-based chain target exists.

A detail worth keeping, because it made the isolated test possible with no code change at all: trunk_gate compares
against whatever is named l_out-0.bin in its directory and takes its input from hc_init.bin.  So a directory holding
the ORACLE'S l_out-1 under the name l_out-0.bin and the ORACLE'S l_out-0 under the name hc_init.bin turns the tool
into a block-1 gate: input and reference both the oracle's, block 1's weights, and the comparison is then genuinely
apples-to-apples.  The naming convention is a limitation of the tool and is worked around rather than hidden.

STATE: two blocks verified to the fp32 floor, both isolated and chained.  What remains is unchanged in kind and smaller
in scope - the pack-based chain target (WeightTable/arena/NativeDense, 45 binds, FileExpertSource, native_fmt from the
pack's own types, 34 KDA states and 11 MLA caches), the head, and the gate argmax == 12089.  The MLA path has still
never executed at all, and block 3 is the first MLA layer.

THE MLA PATH IS VERIFIED AT THE KERNEL LEVEL - the branch that had never executed

    $PY tools/glm5_mla_reference.py --gguf $GGUF --layer 3 --tokens 1 --raw-fixture /home/peb/moredata/glm5-mla-l3.raw
        -> 470,075,420 bytes, layer 3, cache 1 latent
    ./build-glm5/glm_mla_parity /home/peb/moredata/glm5-mla-l3.raw
        qr     max abs 5.722e-06  (1.97e-06 of scale)   PASS
        kv     max abs 2.861e-06  (1.88e-06 of scale)   PASS
        qcur   max abs 4.470e-06  (9.66e-07 of scale)   PASS
        attn   max abs 0.000e+00  (0.00e+00 of scale)   PASS   <- bit-exact
        out    max abs 1.836e-05  (4.00e-06 of scale)   PASS
        invariants: with a 1-entry cache every head returns that entry (worst 0.000e+00)
        glm_mla_parity: 0 failures

Block 3 is the first MLA layer and the MLA branch of the trunk loop had never executed in any form.  It has now, and
the kernel agrees with the oracle at five stages - including attn BIT-EXACT and the 1-entry-cache invariant, which is
the one MLA-specific claim that a tolerance cannot cover: with a single cached latent every head must return that entry,
and it does to 0.000e+00.

The gate and its fixture builder both already existed (glm_mla_parity, glm5_mla_reference.py --raw-fixture); what was
missing was the fixture, which is one command.  Recorded because the same is true of the routed expert path:

    $PY tools/glm5_moe_reference.py --gguf $GGUF --raw-fixture <path>
    ./build-glm5/glm_moe_parity <path>

which is the remaining kernel-level gap now that MLA is closed.

WHAT REMAINS UNTESTED, stated precisely, because the distinction matters after this result:

  * the MLA KERNEL is verified; the MLA WIRING is not.  glm_trunk_forward's is_mla branch - which decides that MLA
    gets the site norm applied and KDA does not (the double-norm fix), and which threads the latent cache and its
    growing length - has still never run, and cannot until a block whose attention is MLA can be built, which means
    the pack;
  * the routed expert path (blocks 3..44) has never run in the loop either; its kernel gate is the command above;
  * the head and the token gate argmax == 12089 remain.

STATE: two blocks verified to the fp32 floor, isolated and chained; the KDA and MLA kernels both verified against the
oracle; the dense FFN and both dequantizers bit-exact or at the floor.  Nothing in the port is known to be wrong; what
is missing is execution of the parts that need the pack.

THE ROUTED EXPERT PATH IS NOT VERIFIED - glm_moe_parity returns FAILURES, and it is right to

    $PY tools/glm5_moe_reference.py --gguf $GGUF --layer 3 --raw-fixture /home/peb/moredata/glm5-moe-l3.raw
        -> 910,755,036 bytes, 8 experts (ids [45, 25, 117, 180, 98, 55, 9, 88])
    ./build-glm5/glm_moe_parity /home/peb/moredata/glm5-moe-l3.raw
        clamp exercised: 0 pre-activation(s) past exp 10 / shexp 10, max |gate| 3.372   NO
        glm_moe_parity: FAILURES (the clamp is not covered)

Read carefully, this is NOT a numerical mismatch report: the gate produced no per-stage max-abs lines at all, because it
refuses at the clamp-coverage check before claiming anything.  The fixture's largest pre-activation is 3.372 against a
limit of 10, so the clamp is inert and the gate cannot catch a port that omits it - and rather than pass vacuously it
reports FAILURES.  That is the correct behaviour and the same one this port demanded of the FFN gate, whose block-1 run
returned INCONCLUSIVE for exactly this reason.

SO THE HONEST STATE OF THE ROUTED EXPERT KERNEL IS: UNVERIFIED.  Not "passed", not "failed" - the gate will not give a
verdict on a fixture that cannot exercise the term it tests, and I am recording it that way rather than reading the
absence of a numerical failure as a success.  This is the third time in this port that a green-looking result had to be
refused on these grounds (the layer_weights_gate that passed while skipping 23 of 25 checks; the FFN INCONCLUSIVE; now
this), and it is why the rule exists.

HOW TO CLOSE IT, taken from the gate that did solve this: ffn_gate demonstrated its clamp by running the same fixture
with the limit FORCED to 0, producing a 15,000x failure and proving the clamp is wired.  The same approach applies here
- run glm_moe_parity with clamp_exp/clamp_shexp overridden low enough that the fixture's own pre-activations cross them,
require the numerical stages to then FAIL, and only then trust a PASS at the artifact's real limits.  Until that is
done, blocks 3..44 - every routed block, 42 of the 45 - rest on a kernel whose parity gate has never returned a verdict.

STATE AFTER THIS ENTRY, complete and without inflation:

    verified:       block 0 and block 1 end to end, isolated and chained, at the fp32 floor (1.2e-05 to 1.6e-05 of rms)
                    the KDA kernel (12/12, four layers)
                    the MLA kernel (5 stages, attn bit-exact, 1-entry-cache invariant)
                    hc_pre, hc_post, the leading dense FFN
                    dequant_q5_K and dequant_q6_K, bit-exact
                    the weight mapping, 25 checks
    not verified:   the routed expert kernel (this entry)
                    the MLA and MoE WIRING inside glm_trunk_forward - neither branch has ever run
                    everything past block 2, which needs the pack
                    the head, and argmax == 12089

THE ROUTED EXPERT PATH IS VERIFIED, AND THE CLAMP IS PROVEN WIRED - the previous entry is superseded

Run A - the fixture at the artifact's real limits, clamp inert, arithmetic compared anyway:

    ids      PASS   got 45,25,117,180,98,55,9,88   want 45,25,117,180,98,55,9,88   (exact, all eight)
    weights  max abs 1.192e-07 (= 3.54e-07 of scale)   worst element rel 3.867e-07   PASS
    moe      max abs 1.699e-06 (= 2.24e-06 of scale)   worst element rel 2.093e-05   PASS
    shexp    max abs 6.855e-07 (= 1.71e-06 of scale)   worst element rel 1.621e-05   PASS
    out      max abs 1.490e-06 (= 1.56e-06 of scale)   worst element rel 1.188e-05   PASS
    invariant sum(weights) 2.500000 (== w_scale 2.5, i.e. normalised THEN scaled) PASS  all positive PASS
              ids distinct PASS  in range PASS
    glm_moe_parity: 0 failures

Run B - THE SAME CONTENT with only the header's two limits changed 10 -> 3.0, so that the fixture finally crosses
them (5 pre-activations past) and the clamp is covered:

    weights  PASS  (unchanged - the router is not clamped, so lowering the clamp must not touch it)
    shexp    FAIL  worst element rel 6.122e-02
    out      FAIL  worst element rel 2.401e-02
    glm_moe_parity: FAILURES

THE PAIR IS THE VERIFICATION.  A alone would say "the arithmetic agrees" while leaving open whether the clamp is
implemented at all, since a port that omitted it would produce the same numbers on a fixture that never reaches it.
B closes that: lowering the limit changes EXACTLY the terms the clamp governs - shexp and out - and leaves the router
weights bit-unchanged, which is the signature of a clamp that is wired rather than absent.  Both halves are required and
neither alone is a verification, which is why the tool now refuses neither and reports both.

WHAT MADE IT POSSIBLE was a one-line change to glm_moe_parity: it returned FAILURES when the clamp was uncovered,
BEFORE comparing anything, so the routed path - 42 of the 45 blocks - had no numerical verdict at all.  It now follows
the convention ffn_gate established: compare the arithmetic regardless, and REPORT the term the fixture cannot reach
instead of letting it suppress the verdict.  A PASS with an inert clamp means "the arithmetic agrees at the real limits
with the clamp unexercised", which is exactly what it says on the line above it.

SUPERSEDED: the previous entry recorded this kernel as UNVERIFIED and said the gate "refuses at the clamp-coverage
check before claiming anything".  That was an accurate description of the tool's behaviour and an accurate statement
that no verdict existed; it is superseded because the tool now produces the verdict, and both halves above exist.  The
unverified reading was the right one to write down at the time - the mistake would have been to read the absence of a
numerical failure as a pass.

STATE NOW: every KERNEL in the chain is verified against the oracle - KDA (12/12), MLA (5 stages, attn bit-exact,
1-entry-cache invariant), the routed expert path (this entry), the dense FFN, hc_pre, hc_post, dequant_q5_K and
dequant_q6_K.  What remains unverified is WIRING, not arithmetic: the is_mla branch and the routed branch of
glm_trunk_forward have still never executed, because a block that uses either needs the pack.

THE HEAD EXISTS AND IS VERIFIED - AND THE GATE IS PROVEN SENSITIVE TO THE STEP IT TESTS

The head had no implementation in this engine at all.  Two things were added:

    glm_stage_head_mean_norm(hc, hc_streams, n_embd, output_norm, hidden, err)     src/core/glm_layer.cpp + header
    tools/head_gate.cpp + a CMake target

and the reference's own head sequence is (glm5_full_run.py:158-178):

    cur = HC.hc_mean(np.transpose(inpL, (1, 0, 2)))      # the MEAN OVER THE FOUR HC STREAMS
    cur = DRV.rms_norm_ne0(cur, output_norm.weight)
    x   = cur[:, -1]                                     # the LAST prompt token
    logits = output.weight @ x                           # the tied projection, then argmax

    engine hidden: rms 1.3855285
    oracle hidden: rms 1.3855285
    worst absolute difference 4.76837e-07   relative to rms 3.44155e-07   ratio 1
    [sensitivity] skipping the mean would give worst 0.659243 - this gate separates the two by 1382533x
    HEAD GATE: PASS

THE STEP WORTH TESTING IS NOT THE NORM, IT IS THE AVERAGING, and the gate says so in numbers.  The trunk's output is
four hyper-connection streams and the reference consumes their MEAN; a head that omitted that line - one line, no shape
change - would project a single stream and emit plausible logits from the wrong vector.  The sensitivity line measures
exactly that alternative: 0.659243 against the 4.77e-07 the correct code produces, a separation of 1.4 million times.
A gate that cannot distinguish the step it exists to test is not a gate, and this one can, by construction rather than
by claim.

The trunk does NOT have to be 45 blocks deep for this: the head consumes whatever the trunk produced, so a 3-block run
verifies the same code and the oracle writes its hidden state for any --blocks value.  The 3-block run's own
"argmax = 6196 expected 12089 MISMATCH" is therefore EXPECTED and meaningless - the head is being applied to a
three-block hidden state - and it is recorded here so it is not mistaken for a defect, exactly as the 1-block run's
mismatch was.

WHAT THE HEAD STILL LACKS, and it is the last piece of it: the output projection (a 154,880 x 4,096 matvec against the
tied output.weight) and the argmax.  Those can be verified INDEPENDENTLY of the engine's trunk, using the oracle's full
45-block hidden state plus output.weight - which is worth doing because it would establish argmax == 12089 without
waiting for the pack-based chain.  The full 45-block oracle run is in progress and writes both l_out-{0..44} and the
hidden state.

ALSO WORTH RECORDING for whoever picks this up: output_norm.weight is NOT in the first GGUF shard, and gguf-py's
GGUFReader does not take a list of shards in this version.  The multi-shard reader that DOES work is
glm5_kda_reference.Model, which make_block_fixture.py already uses -
`import glm5_kda_reference as K; K.Model(sorted(glob('*.gguf'))[0])` - and it opens the sibling shards itself.  Both
failures were hit here before finding it.

THE HEAD IS COMPLETE AND VERIFIED, TOKEN INCLUDED

    glm_stage_head_project(W, vocab, n_embd, hidden, argmax, best, err)      src/core/glm_layer.cpp + header
    tools/head_gate.cpp extended with the projection and the token check

    engine hidden: rms 1.3855285   ==   oracle hidden: rms 1.3855285      (worst 4.76837e-07)
    [sensitivity] skipping the mean would give worst 0.659243 - this gate separates the two by 1382533x
    projection: w_output.bin holds 154880 rows of 4096 = 2,537,553,920 bytes
    engine argmax 6196 (logit 10.3326) over 154880 rows
    expected      6196
    token PASS
    HEAD GATE: PASS

So the whole head pipeline - mean over the four HC streams, output_norm, the 154,880-row tied output projection, and
the greedy argmax - reproduces the oracle's own token.  Nothing in the head is now unverified.

WHY 6196 AND NOT 12089, and why that is the right thing to do first: the head consumes whatever the trunk produced, so
a 3-BLOCK hidden state still exercises every line of it, and the oracle prints its own argmax for that depth.  Verifying
the projection against 6196 establishes the code path BEFORE the full-model token is available; when the 45-block run
finishes and the engine disagrees on 12089, the projection cannot be the reason.  The expected token is an argument
precisely so the same tool serves both.  (The full 45-block oracle run is in progress and writes both l_out-{0..44} and
its hidden state; pointing head_gate at that directory with 12089 is the real gate and needs no further code.)

ONE DELIBERATE EXCEPTION TO THE STREAMING DISCIPLINE, recorded as such: the projection takes a MATERIALISED
[154880][4096] float matrix rather than the quantized-blob path everything else in this port uses.  output.weight is
634,388,480 values - 2.54 GB in fp32 - and it is the model's last operation, touched exactly once per token either way,
so streaming it would buy nothing at inference time and would cost a quantized-matvec path that does not exist.  The
accumulation is in DOUBLE, matching the reference, because the argmax compares 154,880 sums of 4,096 terms and a float
accumulator is enough to move the winner when two logits are close - and the token is the entire observable output.

STATE OF THE CHAIN, complete: every kernel verified against the oracle (KDA 12/12, MLA 5 stages with attn bit-exact,
routed experts with the clamp proven wired, dense FFN, hc_pre, hc_post, both dequantizers, the weight mapping); blocks
0 and 1 verified end to end, isolated and chained; the head verified complete including its token.  What remains is
WIRING THAT NEEDS THE PACK - the is_mla and routed branches of glm_trunk_forward, which have still never executed - and
then the 45-block run and argmax == 12089, whose head half is now known to work.

THE TOKEN GATE PASSES: THE ENGINE'S HEAD PRODUCES 12089 ON THE FULL 45-BLOCK HIDDEN STATE

The full oracle run completed: blocks 0..44, MLA at 23, 27, 31, 35, 39, 43, and

    logits argmax = 12089   expected 12089   MATCH
    |hidden| 82.6806

and head_gate, pointed at that run's l_out-44 with w_output.bin and its saved hidden state:

    engine hidden: rms 1.29188498
    oracle hidden: rms 1.29188499
    worst absolute difference 4.76837e-07   relative to rms 3.69102e-07   ratio 0.999999987
    [sensitivity] skipping the mean would give worst 3.06072 - this gate separates the two by 6418800x
    projection: w_output.bin holds 154880 rows of 4096
    engine argmax 12089 (logit 17.1146) over 154880 rows
    expected      12089
    token PASS
    HEAD GATE: PASS

THE CAVEAT, stated precisely because it is the whole distinction: the hidden state fed to the head came from the
ORACLE's trunk (l_out-44), not the engine's.  So what this establishes is that the engine's head - mean over streams,
output_norm, the 154,880-row projection, the argmax - is correct AT FULL DEPTH and produces the model's token.  What it
does NOT establish is the engine's own trunk at 45 blocks, which still needs the pack.  The gate is now the real one,
though: when the engine's trunk produces its own l_out-44, this exact command is the end-to-end test and no new code is
needed for it.

ALSO FROM THE FULL RUN, and it revises something recorded earlier: the clamps ARE exercised at depth -
{'exp_gate': 5, 'exp_up': 3, 'shexp_gate': 2, 'shexp_up': 15, 'calls': 1890}.  Twenty-five crossings across 1,890
calls.  So the earlier glm_moe_parity result - the fixture that could not reach the clamp - was a limitation of THAT
FIXTURE, not evidence that the clamp is unreachable in this model.  The two runs together are what the port needed: the
gate showed the clamp is WIRED (by lowering the limit and watching exactly the terms it governs move), and the full
model shows the clamp is REACHED.  Neither alone would have said both things.

SMALL GOTCHA WORTH RECORDING: np.save appends ".npy" when the filename does not already end in it, so
--save-hidden <dir>/hidden.bin wrote <dir>/hidden.bin.npy.  The run reported the path it was given, not the path it
wrote, which cost one failed lookup here.

STATE: every kernel verified against the oracle; blocks 0 and 1 end to end, isolated and chained, at the fp32 floor; the
head verified complete AND producing the correct token at full depth.  Unverified: the engine's own 45-block trunk,
which needs the pack - the is_mla and routed branches of glm_trunk_forward have still never executed.

BLOCK 3'S DENSE TENSORS ARE FETCHED - the MLA + routed block is now half-fixture

21 of 21 tensors fetched from the artifact to /home/peb/moredata/glm5-block-l3 by name, and their SIZES independently
confirm the MLA geometry that glm_mla_parity printed (64 heads x 256, kv_lora 512, q_lora 1536):

    w_hc_attn_fn     393,216   = 24 x 16,384        w_hc_attn_base      24        w_hc_attn_scale  3
    w_attn_norm        4,096                        w_hc_ffn_fn    393,216        w_hc_ffn_base   24
    w_hc_ffn_scale         3                        w_ffn_norm       4,096
    w_q_a          6,291,456   = 1,536 x 4,096      w_q_a_norm       1,536
    w_q_b         25,165,824   = 16,384 x 1,536     w_kv_a       2,097,152   = 512 x 4,096
    w_kv_a_norm          512                        w_k_b        8,388,608   = 64 x 512 x 256
    w_v_b          8,388,608                        w_wo        67,108,864   = 4,096 x 16,384
    w_router       1,179,648   = 288 x 4,096        w_probs_b          288
    w_shexp_gate/up 8,388,608  = 2,048 x 4,096      w_shexp_down 8,388,608  = 4,096 x 2,048

Every one matches a shape the engine's own structs declare, so the names in glm_fill_layer_weights' Names struct are
not merely plausible for an MLA layer - they resolve, at the right widths, in the artifact.  The one that matters most is
shexp: ff = 2,048 for the SHARED expert, the same width as a routed expert and NOT the dense block's 12,288, which is
why GlmTrunkLayerWeights carries a separate shexp geometry rather than assuming one.

WHAT IS STILL MISSING FOR BLOCK 3, and it is only the routed half: the QUANTIZED EXPERT BLOBS.  The dense tensors above
are floats and come from the artifact; the experts are ggml blocks and come from the pack's experts.bin (86.41 GB) via
FileExpertSource::open(pack_dir, n_layers, n_expert) - the interface whose blob(layer, expert), begin_layer(layer, ids,
k) and pinned/reads members were read earlier.  With those, plus native_fmt(gu_type, d_type, 4,096, 2,048) built from the
types the pack's own native_experts.txt carries for layer 3, glm_stage_moe_native has everything it needs.

SO THE BLOCK-3 GATE IS: bind these 21 tensors to a GlmBoundBlock, run glm_fill_layer_weights with layer = 3 (which
selects MLA + routed by itself - no forcing), fill in moe_g {n_embd 4,096, n_expert 288, n_used 8, ff 2,048}, shexp_g
and the shexp triple, open FileExpertSource on the pack, set moe_fmt and blob_fn, and call glm_trunk_forward with 1
layer.  Its input is the oracle's l_out-2 and its reference is the oracle's l_out-3 - both already on disk from the full
45-block run, so no new oracle work is needed and the comparison is apples-to-apples by construction.  That single run
would exercise BOTH branches of the loop that have never executed, and it is the last thing standing between this port
and a 45-block run.

THE ROUTED PATH ACCEPTS THE PACK'S REAL TYPES, AND ITS BLOB GEOMETRY MATCHES THE PACK'S TO THE BYTE

The pack's native_experts.txt is fully specified - "layer gu_type d_type offset blob_bytes gate_off up_off down_off
[shard] (n_expert 288, total 86406856704)" - and its very first data line is a surprise worth recording:

    3  16 18 0 7536640 2365811904 2999168192 1434086592  GLM-5.3-Flash-UD-IQ1_S-00002-of-00003.gguf
    4  19 18 2170552320 6488064 ...

Layer 3's experts are gu_type 16 (IQ2_XXS) and d_type 18 (IQ3_XXS); layer 4 is 19 (IQ1_S) and 18.  Those are NOT the
types this port's own dequantizers handle - dequant_q5_K (13), dequant_q6_K (14) and dequant_q8_0 (8) are what the
BIND-TIME path uses for the dense tensors, and none of them covers an IQ type.  The routed path is not that path: it is
ggml-cpu's, and native_fmt consults ggml's own type traits.  So the question was whether it accepts what the pack
carries, and the answer is measured:

    native_experts_available: yes
    layer 3 (16,18)        OK  gu_act 15 d_act 15  bytes 7536640  act_bytes 4672  h_bytes 2336  gu_row 1056  d_row 784
    layer 4 (19,18)        OK  gu_act 15 d_act 15  bytes 6488064  act_bytes 4672  h_bytes 2336  gu_row 800   d_row 784
    for reference (13,14)  OK  gu_act 15 d_act 15  bytes 18415616
    the pack declares blob_bytes 7,536,640 for layer 3

TWO THINGS THIS ESTABLISHES, and the second is the one that matters:

  * the native path ACCEPTS all three of the pack's real types, so the routed expert path is not blocked on quantizer
    work.  gu_act and d_act are 15 = Q8_K, the vector-dot type both need, and act_bytes 4672 / h_bytes 2336 fit inside
    the pool buffers - which are sized 4096*40/32+64 = 5184 and 2048*40/32+64 = 2624 BY THE BUILD FLAGS this branch
    already uses (-DSTRATA_MODEL_H=4096 -DSTRATA_MODEL_FF=2048).  That is the second independent confirmation that
    those two flags are right, after the fixtures;
  * native_fmt's own `bytes` EQUALS the pack's declared blob_bytes - 7,536,640 for layer 3 and 6,488,064 for layer 4,
    both exact.  The descriptor the engine computes and the geometry the pack declares AGREE TO THE BYTE on two
    independent layers, which means an expert blob will be read at the correct stride.  A mismatch there would not have
    looked like an error: it would have produced smoothly wrong expert outputs and been hunted as a numerical bug,
    which is exactly the class of failure this port has spent the session learning to catch before it costs a day.

ALSO RECORDED, because it cost a failed link here and will cost another: libstrata_kernels_cpu.a needs ggml's static
libraries, and they are at

    build-glm5/ggml/src/libggml-cpu.a   and   build-glm5/ggml/src/libggml-base.a

NOT under build-glm5/_deps (nothing is there).  The probe linked with
`libstrata_kernels_cpu.a libggml-cpu.a libggml-base.a -lpthread -lm`, and that is the line the block-3 gate and the
chain target will need.

STATE: the routed expert path's plumbing is now validated against the pack's real geometry, the dense half of block 3 is
fixtures on disk with its shapes confirmed, and the oracle's l_out-2 / l_out-3 give that block's input and reference.
What remains is the block-3 gate itself - the tool that binds those 21 tensors, opens FileExpertSource, fills moe_fmt and
blob_fn, and calls glm_trunk_forward - which would be the first execution of the is_mla AND routed branches.

glm_trunk_forward CAN NOW START AT A LAYER, WHICH IS WHAT A BLOCK-3 RUN NEEDS

    glm_trunk_forward(..., int first_layer = 0)

The loop previously dispatched on ITS OWN COUNTER, so a one-layer run always used layer 0 - KDA and dense.  Every
dispatch (glm_attention_is_mla / glm_ffn_is_dense), every weight lookup and the state maps are keyed by the ARTIFACT's
layer number, so a run starting at block 3 must say so or it is dispatched as a KDA dense block and the provider is
asked for tensors block 3 does not have.  The loop now computes `const int layer = first_layer + i;` once and uses it
for all of them.  Defaulted, so the existing callers are unchanged - and they were re-run to prove it rather than
assumed:

    block 0          worst 7.35745e-08   relative 1.23591e-05   ratio 1   TRUNK GATE: PASS
    block 1 chained  worst 1.16415e-07   relative 1.60491e-05   ratio 1   TRUNK GATE: PASS
    head, full depth engine argmax 12089   token PASS                     HEAD GATE: PASS

Identical numbers to before the change, which is the point: a refactor of verified code has to be re-verified, and
"it cannot have changed anything" is not evidence.

WHY THIS WAS THE LAST BLOCKER ON A BLOCK-3 RUN: block 3 is the first MLA layer AND is routed, so both branch conditions
differ from block 0's - and with the loop able to start at 3, a one-layer run now exercises the is_mla branch and the
routed branch, neither of which has ever executed.  Everything that run needs is already in place and verified: the 21
dense tensors fetched and shape-checked, the routed descriptor matching the pack's blob_bytes to the byte for layer 3,
FileExpertSource's interface read, the oracle's l_out-2 as input and l_out-3 as reference, and the ggml link line
recorded (build-glm5/ggml/src/libggml-{cpu,base}.a).

THE BLOCK-3 GATE EXISTS AND ITS FIRST BLOCKER IS A REAL ONE: THE PACK'S LAYER NUMBERING

tools/block3_gate.cpp + a CMake target.  It binds block 3's 21 fetched tensors, opens FileExpertSource on the pack,
builds the descriptor with native_fmt from the pack's OWN types, runs glm_trunk_forward with first_layer = 3, and
compares against the oracle's l_out-3.  Three things it establishes before it stops:

    layer 3 experts: gu_type 16, d_type 18, pack blob_bytes 7536640, descriptor bytes 7536640  (MATCH)

so the type reading and the descriptor are right, and it gets far enough to open the pack.

THE BLOCKER, and it is the kind that has to be decided rather than tuned:

    the pack's native_experts.txt lists layers 3..45 with ABSOLUTE layer numbers (GLM's first three blocks are dense
    and have no experts), while expert_layout_load wants a dense 0..n_layers-1 set.

        n_layers = 46  -> parses, then "the native expert layout is invalid at layer 0"   (0, 1, 2 have no entry)
        n_layers = 43  -> "native_experts.txt: a malformed line: 43 16 18 ..."            (layer 43 > 43 layers)

Both refusals are correct given their premise.  So this is a decision about how the pack REPRESENTS a layer with no
experts - a row with blob_bytes 0, a flag, or a leading-dense offset in the loader - and not a number to try until
something runs.

AND THE TEMPTING SHORTCUT IS THE DANGEROUS ONE.  Passing 43 would make the layout index ROW ORDER rather than layer
number, so layer 3 would be served layer 6's experts: valid pointers, correctly-sized blobs, plausible numbers,
silently wrong - and the gate would even be able to produce a comparison to argue about.  It is refused here for the
same reason the port refuses every other plausible-wrong answer.  46 is what the tool passes, because that value
reaches the real blocker instead of a parse error, and the gate halts on it rather than proceeding on a guess.

WHAT IS *NOT* BLOCKED, and it is most of the gate: the 21 tensors are fetched and shape-checked, the MLA geometry is
set from the artifact's own numbers, the descriptor matches the pack's blob_bytes exactly, FileExpertSource's interface
is wired, the oracle's l_out-2 and l_out-3 are the input and reference, and the tool builds and runs.  One decision
about representing expertless layers stands between this and the first execution of the is_mla and routed branches.

THE BLOCK-3 GATE RUNS: THE is_mla AND ROUTED BRANCHES EXECUTED FOR THE FIRST TIME

    layer 3 experts: gu_type 16, d_type 18, pack blob_bytes 7536640, descriptor bytes 7536640  (MATCH)
    loaded expert layout: n_layers 46, n_expert 288
    l_out-2 (block 3's input) ne=[4096,4,5,1] 81920 floats
    5 token(s) in the dump; running token 4 (the last), block 3, MLA + routed
    engine l_out: rms 0.0571010654
    oracle l_out: rms 0.0583536194
    worst absolute difference 0.0582634   relative to rms 0.998453   ratio 0.97853511
    BLOCK3 GATE: FAIL

THE BUG THAT BLOCKED IT WAS IN THE ENGINE, AND IT WAS THIS PORT'S FIRST PACK-FACING DEFECT:

FileExpertSource::open validated a CONTIGUOUS 0..n_layers-1 set and never consulted ExpertLayout::first_layer -
which exists for exactly this case, is set by the loader from the pack's own lowest layer, and whose comment names this
model ("A model whose first blocks are a dense MLP stem (GLM-5.3-Flash: blocks 0-2) starts its native_experts.txt
here").  Layers 0-2 hold offset ~0ull and bytes 0 BY DESIGN, so open() refused a layout that was correct, with "the
native expert layout is invalid at layer 0".  Fixed by starting the validation at first_layer and seeding the running
offset from it; layer_offsets/layer_blob_bytes stay zeroed for the stem, as the layout's own comment says they should
("no consumer may ask this table for them").  This is worth recording because it is the first defect found on the
PACK-FACING side rather than in the GLM arithmetic: nothing in this port had ever loaded a native pack whose model has
a dense stem, so the path had never been exercised.

THE REMAINING FAIL IS THE GATE'S OWN DOING, and the diagnosis is specific rather than a shrug.  The rms values are close
(ratio 0.978) while the worst elementwise difference is 99.8% of rms - the signature of a block that computes the right
KIND of thing and differs structurally, not one that is numerically off.  The cause: the gate feeds token 4 to a FRESH
MLA cache.  In the oracle's run, MLA layers accumulate a latent per token, so its token-4 output attends to the latents
of tokens 0-4, while the gate's single call gives the MLA branch one cell and it attends only to itself.  The KDA blocks
never exposed this because KDA carries its state as a matrix the loop threads correctly; MLA carries it as a GROWING
CACHE, and the gate never grew it.

So the fix is in the gate and it is small: run the five tokens in order, threading the MLA cache so it accumulates
exactly as the oracle's does, and compare the LAST token's output against l_out-3.  The oracle dump already holds every
token, every input and every reference, so nothing new is needed - and this is also the first exercise of the MLA cache
across tokens, which is the remaining untested mechanism even in the KDA-verified blocks.

THE MLA CACHE WAS NEVER POPULATED - AND FIXING IT COLLAPSED THE BLOCK-3 ERROR BY 16x

    before:  engine rms 0.0571010654   oracle 0.0583536194   worst 0.0582634   (99.8% of rms)
    after:   engine rms 0.0583401184   oracle 0.0583536194   worst 0.00354951  (6.1% of rms)  ratio 0.999768634

THE BUG, and the evidence that found it was the identical numbers rather than the failing comparison: running five
tokens with a cache that grew 1 -> 2 -> 3 -> 4 -> 5 produced output BIT-IDENTICAL to the one-token run.  That is only
possible if the attention sees the same latents either way, and attending over N identical ZERO latents gives the same
answer for any N - so the cache was not being written at all.  The cause was in glm_trunk_forward's MLA branch:

    kernels::glm::MlaIntermediates want;
    want.kv = cache + cells * kv_lora;              // a place for this token's latent
    glm_stage_mla(w, g, xn, cells + 1, cache, attn_out, err);   // ... which was never passed

glm_stage_mla took no MlaIntermediates, so mla_forward ran without a destination and the latent it computes to attend
with was discarded.  Every token therefore attended over a cache of zeros, and the KDA blocks could not have exposed
it: KDA's state is a matrix the loop threads, MLA's is a cache the loop had to append to and could not.  Fixed by
giving glm_stage_mla an optional MlaIntermediates* and passing &want from the loop.

WHY IT WAS INVISIBLE UNTIL NOW, stated plainly: the MLA KERNEL is bit-exact - 5 stages, attn at 0.000e+00, the
1-entry-cache invariant - because that gate passes a cache that ALREADY holds the token's latent, exactly as the
oracle does.  Every kernel gate in this port hands the kernel the state it needs; none of them can see whether the
ASSEMBLY builds that state.  This is the same lesson as the double-norm bug, in a different place: a test that verifies
a part cannot see a wiring error.

STILL NOT PASSING, and honestly: 6.1% of rms is far above the fp32 floor the KDA blocks reach (1.2e-05), so something
else in this block is still wrong.  It is now a bisection rather than a search - the two candidates are the remaining
MLA detail (whether mla_forward attends over the cache INCLUDING the latent it just wrote, or only the entries already
there) and the routed expert path (blob selection per expert).  The dump holds every intermediate the oracle computed
for this block, so the next step is to compare the MLA stage's own output against the oracle's.

REGRESSION CHECK: the change touched only the MLA path, so the KDA blocks do not call it - but the two verified gates
were re-run rather than assumed, and both are unchanged.

BLOCK 3'S REMAINING 6%: THE FIRST TWO CANDIDATES ARE ELIMINATED BY READING, NOT BY GUESSING

After the MLA-cache fix the block sits at worst 0.00354951 = 6.1% of rms, against the 1.2e-05 the KDA blocks reach.
Two specific explanations were tested and both are dead:

  1. THE ATTEND ORDER IS RIGHT.  The suspicion was that mla_forward attends over the passed cache BEFORE writing the
     current token's latent, which would make the loop's `cells + 1` pass a stale entry.  Reading glm_mla.cpp:
         line 71  rms_norm_inplace(kv.data(), kv_lora, w.kv_a_norm);
         line 72  if (want.kv) std::copy(kv.begin(), kv.end(), want.kv);      <- written FIRST
         line 79  for (int h = 0; h < n_head; ++h) { ... }                    <- attention AFTER
     so the latent is in the cache before the attention reads it, and the 1-entry-cache invariant in the parity gate
     agrees: a cache of one holds that token's latent, which is the convention the loop now satisfies.

  2. THE MoE GEOMETRY IS RIGHT, INCLUDING THE SCALE.  MoeGeometry's defaults are n_embd 4096, n_expert 288, n_used 8,
     ff 2048, w_scale 2.5f, norm_w true, clamp 10 - and 2.5 is exactly what glm_moe_parity printed from the artifact
     ("scale 2.5"), so the combine weights are scaled as the model requires and the gate sets the rest explicitly.
     SwiGLU clamp 10 is the documented value for every layer of this model.

WHAT IS LEFT, with the search narrowed rather than guessed at.  The block's four parts are hc_pre -> MLA -> hc_post ->
hc_pre -> routed MoE -> hc_post, and of those: the hc sites are verified in blocks 0 and 1, the MLA kernel is bit-exact,
the routed kernel is verified with its clamp proven wired, and the pack geometry matches native_fmt to the byte.  The
remaining candidates are therefore the things that only exist in the ASSEMBLY of an MLA + routed block:

  * whether the loop's MLA cache is per LAYER as the oracle's is (its `latents: dict[int, list[np.ndarray]]` is keyed
    by layer), or whether the gate's single cache slot is being reused in a way that mixes two layers' latents.  The
    gate has ONE slot and first_layer = 3 runs ONE layer, so this should not bite here - but it is the next thing to
    check rather than assume;
  * whether gl_stage_moe_native is given the same expert ids the oracle routed to.  The oracle's MoE fixture gate
    compared ids exactly for layer 3 (45,25,117,180,98,55,9,88) but with the ORACLE's own input; the block feeds it the
    block's normed input, so a router disagreement would show up here and nowhere else.

THE NEXT MEASUREMENT is to have the runner dump block 3's per-stage intermediates (it already dumps l_out-N; adding
attn_output and ffn_out for one layer is a few lines) and compare the engine's attention-site and FFN outputs against
them separately.  That splits 6% into "before the FFN" or "the FFN" in a single run instead of by argument.

THE EXISTING BLOCK-3 STAGE DUMPS ARE NOT A VALID REFERENCE, AND THAT IS NOW PROVEN RATHER THAN ASSUMED

The plan was to bisect block 3's remaining 6% against the stage families already on disk (hc_attn_pre-3, attn_norm-3,
ffn_norm-3, ffn_out-3, hc_attn_post-3, l_out-3).  Two measurements killed that shortcut, and both are recorded here so
the next attempt does not spend a run discovering them:

  1. glm5_block_driver.py, the tool that writes the stage families, ONLY DOES KDA BLOCKS.  Running it for four blocks
     stops with "block 3 is an MLA block; this driver only does KDA blocks so far", so the block-3 stages on disk came
     from a DIFFERENT tool - one whose MLA path is a separate implementation from the oracle's.

  2. THAT TOOL'S BLOCK 3 DOES NOT AGREE WITH THE ORACLE.  tools/check_stage_refs.py compares a dump's l_out-3 elementwise
     against the oracle's own l_out-3 - a source that never went through the writer that was fixed, so agreement would
     be evidence and not a tautology:

         oracle l_out-3  ne=[4096,4,5,1]  81920 floats
         driver l_out-3  ne=[4096,4,5,1]  81920 floats
         worst |driver - oracle| over l_out-3: 0.0219   DISAGREE      (against a driver rms of 0.159, so ~13.8%)

     glm5-ref-dump, ...2, ...3 and ...5 all carry the SAME values (identical rms on every stage), so this is not a
     stale copy problem and re-checking another directory cannot fix it.

WHAT FOLLOWS FROM IT: block 3's stage references must come from the AUTHORITATIVE path - the oracle runner
(glm5_full_run.py) whose full 45-block run is the one that produces token 12089 - extended to dump its own block-3
intermediates, with the fixed writer.  Only that source is definitionally consistent with the output this port is
gated on, and the 13.8% disagreement above is exactly why a convenient-looking reference is worth one cheap check
before it is trusted: a stage-level bisection against a dump that is itself off by 14% would have produced a
confident, wrong answer about which half of the block is broken.

This is the third time this session that verifying the FIXTURE came before using it, and it is the same rule each time:
check that a fixture agrees with itself - or with an independent authority - before checking that the engine agrees
with the fixture.

BLOCK 3'S STAGE REFERENCES NOW COME FROM THE AUTHORITATIVE RUNNER, AND THE REF-DUMP DISAGREEMENT HAS A MECHANISM

glm5_full_run.py - the runner whose full 45-block run is what produces token 12089 - now dumps four stage families in
addition to l_out, for every block, on the same container convention (ne0 fastest):

    attn_output-{il}    the attention site's output, before hc_post
    hc_attn_post-{il}   the attention site's hc_post result
    ffn_norm-{il}       the FFN site's normed input
    ffn_out-{il}        the FFN's output, before hc_post

A four-block run writes them (attn_output-3, ffn_norm-3, ffn_out-3, hc_attn_post-3, l_out-3 all present).  These are
the references the bisection needs, and they come from the same code path as the token gate rather than from a second
implementation.  Note the four-block run reports argmax 6196 against the expected 12089 - that is NOT a regression: a
four-block run is not the model, and only the full 45-block run is gated on the token.

AND THE REF-DUMP DISAGREEMENT IS NOW EXPLAINED, which sharpens the warning rather than softening it.  The same
four-block run reports |l_out| 45.5243 for block 3, whose rms over 81,920 values is 0.159 - and the driver dump's
l_out-3 rms is 0.158985821.  The two agree to four digits on a permutation-invariant quantity while their ELEMENTWISE
comparison differs by 0.0219 (13.8%).  That is the signature of the same trap this session has already been bitten by
once: an rms cannot see a layout error.  So the ref dump's block-3 values are most likely the right numbers in a
different ORDER (the five-token axis being the obvious candidate), not wrong numbers - which is exactly why the check
had to be elementwise, and exactly why a bisection run against it would have been misleading in a way that looked
quantitative.

NEXT STEP, now instrumented on one side only: the engine's own stages must be dumpable to complete the comparison.  The
loop needs an optional stage callback - the same shape as the weight provider it already takes - so a gate can receive
(layer, name, data, n) for each stage and compare against the files above.  With that, block 3's 6% splits into "before
the FFN" or "the FFN" in one run: compare attn_output-3 to separate the attention site, and ffn_out-3 to separate the
routed MoE.

THE BISECTION: BLOCK 3'S ERROR IS ENTIRELY INSIDE THE ROUTED MoE STAGE

A stage callback was added to glm_trunk_forward - GlmStageFn, with the two new parameters DEFAULTED to nullptr so a
caller that does not ask for the stream is unaffected, and that is verified rather than asserted: with the callback in
place and unused, trunk_gate and block3_gate reproduced their previous numbers exactly (worst 7.35745e-08, ratio 1;
worst 0.00354951, ratio 0.999768634).  The reference stages come from glm5_full_run.py - the SAME runner that produces
the token gate - so this comparison is not circular.

    attn_output   engine rms 0.387098    oracle rms 0.387099    worst 1.06692e-05  = 2.756e-05 of rms
    hc_attn_post  engine rms 0.0254039   oracle rms 0.0254039   worst 8.33534e-08  = 3.281e-06 of rms
    ffn_norm      engine rms 0.353804    oracle rms 0.353804    worst 9.37283e-06  = 2.649e-05 of rms
    ffn_out       engine rms 0.957992    oracle rms 0.958332    worst 0.0438659    = 4.577e-02 of rms      <- FAILS

READ IT AS FOUR SEPARATE VERDICTS, which is the whole point of building the stream:

  * THE MLA BRANCH IS CORRECT AT THE FP32 FLOOR.  attn_output agrees to 2.8e-05 of rms, with the cache accumulated
    across five tokens.  That is the first numerical verification of the is_mla branch - and of the latent-threading
    fix - against the authoritative reference, and it closes the branch that had never executed before this session.
  * BOTH hc SITES ARE CORRECT: hc_attn_post to 3.3e-06 of rms.
  * THE NORM IS CORRECT: ffn_norm to 2.6e-05 of rms.
  * THE ROUTED MoE STAGE IS THE ONLY WRONG THING in this block.  Its INPUT is verified correct, so the fault is
    inside the stage or in what the loop hands it - and the numbers say which KIND of fault: the rms agrees to 0.04%
    while elements differ by 4.6%, so the mixture has the right magnitude and the wrong members or the wrong weights.
    That is the signature of a routing difference (different experts selected) or of the clamp, NOT of a scale or a
    layout error.

The candidates that remain, in the order worth testing, given that glm_moe_parity already passes for this layer with
the pack's own descriptor matching blob_bytes to the byte (so the kernel, the descriptor and the pack reading are all
verified in isolation):

  1. THE EXPERT IDS.  If the router picks a different top-8 for the block's real input than the oracle does, the
     output has the right kind of magnitude from the wrong experts - exactly what is observed.  The MoE fixture gate
     compared ids EXACTLY, but for the oracle's own input, so a router disagreement on this input would show up here
     and nowhere else.  Testing it needs the engine's ids, which the stage does not currently report.
  2. THE CLAMP.  The oracle's own four-block run counts exp_up 1 and shexp_up 1 - the clamp is active once per
     sixty-odd calls at this depth, and it is the one term whose mis-wiring changes a few elements while leaving the
     rms alone.
  3. THE SHARED EXPERT.  It is added unweighted with its own geometry (2048 wide in this block, not the dense FFN's
     12288) and its own clamp, and it is the part of the stage the parity fixture exercised least.
